Before a store release: create a REAL AdMob rewarded ad unit (placeholder/TODO in `ad-config.ts`), add a GDPR/UMP consent flow and Play Data-Safety ad-ID declaration; create an iOS AdMob app for real iOS ids.

## 5. Architecture and Data Flow
Expo Router owns navigation. Route files in `app/` map URLs/screens to components in `src/screens/`. Root layout mounts cross-cutting lifecycle components once above the navigation stack.

The main app state lives in `useAppStore` and is persisted to AsyncStorage under `swipeclean-free-store`. It stores permission state, selected filters, review/deletion state, compressed results, deletion history, stats, and settings. Store actions call services for native work and update state synchronously after successful results.

The gallery source of truth lives in `useMediaIndexStore` and is persisted to AsyncStorage under `swipeclean-media-index-store`. It stores indexed media by ID plus a sorted ID list, compressibility estimates, full-library savings summary, scan cursors, and scan timestamps. A quick newest-page refresh keeps recently downloaded/captured media available quickly, while the first cooperative full scan fills older months and creates a saved global cleanup estimate. Later full estimate scans are user-triggered from Cleanup.

The compression feature has a separate persisted Zustand store under `swipeclean-compression-store` (debounced storage). It stores compression jobs, media-to-job index, completed media IDs, batches, active job ID, queue, and errors. It writes through to `useAppStore.compressedMedia` after successful compression and may remove originals from the main photo list after verified deletion.

Additional domain stores (one store per domain):
- `useSubscriptionStore` (`swipeclean-subscription-store`) holds the persisted entitlement snapshot, RevenueCat billing UI state/plans, and local Test Store cancel state. The PURE `FeatureAccessService` (`feature-access.service.ts`) computes `isProUser`/`canUseFeature` from feature-flags; React reads it via `useFeatureAccess`, imperative code (e.g. the launch-time scan resume) via `getState()`. `usePaywallStore` is a tiny non-persisted open/close store; one `<ProUpgradeSheet/>` mounted in the root layout renders from it.
- `useCleanupEventsStore` (`swipeclean-cleanup-events-store`) is an append-only, newest-first ledger capped at 2000 events. Reports are derived by PURE selectors in `src/features/advanced-stats/` via `useMemo([events])` — never a store-side aggregate (avoids the fresh-object-selector crash class).
- `useSmartCleanStore` is the PERSISTED Smart Clean scan runner (`swipeclean-smart-clean-results`); `useSmartCleanFeatureCache` (`swipeclean-smart-clean-feature-cache`, debounced) caches per-asset hashes; `useSmartCleanReviewStore` drives the root-mounted review sheet.

Foreground-service coordination: RNBA is a hard singleton. Compression and the Smart Clean scan each have their own thin RNBA wrapper but only one holds the service at a time. The scan acquires it only when free, else runs plain-JS and still checkpoints; compression always has priority and a backgrounded scan yields the service (one-directional) before compression acquires it.

Data flow for media cleanup:
1. `SwipeScreen` waits for store hydration, calls `loadInitialData`, and renders permission/loading/content states.
2. `useAppStore.loadInitialData` checks permission, asks `useMediaIndexStore.refreshNewestPage` for a fast first page, then starts `useMediaIndexStore.startFullScan` only if no full scan has completed yet.
3. `PhotoLibraryService.getPhotosPage` reads media pages through `expo-media-library`; `useMediaIndexStore` maps each page into indexed assets, dedupes by ID, sorts by creation time, stores estimates, and prefetches images.
4. Screens read indexed media through `useIndexedMediaAssets` or `selectIndexedMediaAsset` and derive visible media with `filterPhotosByScope` and local `useMemo`.
5. `usePhotoLibrarySync` listens for media-library changes, app foregrounding, and a 45-second active-app interval; each refresh pulls the newest page without forcing repeated full estimate scans after one has been saved.
6. Swipe/delete actions add IDs to `reviewedPhotoIds` and `markedForDeletion`.
7. If the active swipe scope has media but all items are reviewed or marked, `SwipeScreen` renders a completed state. Start Over calls `useAppStore.restartCurrentSelection`, which resets reviewed IDs for the current month/media type only when no marked-for-deletion items remain in that same scope.
8. Permanent deletion calls `PhotoLibraryService.deletePhotos`; success updates marked queue, reviewed IDs, history, stats, and removes IDs from the media index.

Data flow for compression:
1. Cleanup screens identify compressible assets with `CompressionService.isCompressible`.
2. `createCompressionJobInput` creates queue input with estimated sizes.
3. `useCompressionStore.enqueueCompression` or `enqueueCompressionBatch` persists queued jobs and starts the runner. A batch carries an upfront `originalPolicy` (`"delete" | "keep" | "ask"`, default `"ask"`); `"delete"`/`"keep"` are auto-applied at batch completion and suppress the post-batch decision sheet.
4. `runCompressionJob` wraps compression in `CompressionNotifications.runInForeground`, updates progress, and delegates native compression to `compressMediaJob`. `verifyCompressedOutput` then runs BEFORE `saveToLibrary` and rejects an output that is not smaller than the original (`finalSizeBytes >= knownOriginalSize`), so a no-gain re-encode fails the job instead of writing a larger copy to the gallery.
5. Completed jobs save `CompressedMediaItem` into `useAppStore.compressedMedia` and record to the cleanup-events ledger. (The batch engine and `applyPostCompressionPolicy`/`settings.afterCompressionOriginalPolicy` were removed in the 2026-06-15 foreground redesign — the compress-run screen always asks Keep / Delete original.)
6. Deleting originals uses `PhotoLibraryService.deletePhotos` only after output verification and policy/user decision.

Data flow for background savings estimates:
1. `useMediaIndexStore.startFullScan` reads media pages in chunks and yields briefly between pages.
2. Each page is checked with `CompressionService.isCompressible` and `CompressionService.estimate` while being stored in the index.
3. The index summary persists global scanned count, eligible count, original bytes, compressed bytes, and estimated saved bytes.
4. Cleanup renders the estimate pill from the persisted global index summary instead of reducing over the visible list during render.
5. Cleanup includes an Estimate now/Estimate again button that forces a new full scan and replaces the saved estimate.
6. Already-compressed source IDs are ignored when a scan starts; deleting originals also removes those IDs from the index.

Data flow for Smart Clean (Pro):
1. The screen (or the launch-time auto-resume) ensures a media-index full scan exists, snapshots the indexed assets, and calls `useSmartCleanStore.runScan`.
2. The runner scans in THREE phases (see Feature History 2026-06-15 "single-decode + concurrent pre-pass"): (A) cheap metadata/MD5 detectors (large/screenshots/memes/duplicate-photos) surface in seconds; (B) ONE concurrent single-decode sweep (`prewarmPhotoFeatures`, bounded concurrency via `mapWithConcurrency`) warms each photo's dHash+blur in the persisted `featureCacheApi` — the bulk of the work; (C) the pixel detectors (similar/blurry/duplicate-videos) then run as pure cache hits, so all categories surface together. Runs inside the RNBA foreground service on Android when free; plain foreground JS on iOS. Expensive per-asset work is computed once and reused across detectors and re-scans.
3. After each detector the runner checkpoints (persisted, debounced); `phase` "scanning" persists as "interrupted". On next launch `resumeIfInterrupted` (fired when both stores are ready / on app-foreground) seeds completed detectors from the persisted results and continues at the first unfinished one — provided a library `runSignature` still matches.
4. Results persist compactly (mediaIds only) and are expanded ONCE against the media index on hydrate (pruning deleted items, recomputing counts) into a stable `resultsByKey` the screen subscribes to.
5. Confirmed deletions route through `useAppStore.permanentlyDeleteMarked(ids, { emitDeletionEvent: false })`, then emit a `smartCleanActionConfirmed` event, prune the feature cache, and re-scan from the post-deletion snapshot. Deletion is blocked under non-`granted` (limited) access.

Data flow for feature gating:
1. `SubscriptionSync` in the root layout initializes RevenueCat after the subscription store hydrates and refreshes customer info when the app returns to foreground.
2. RevenueCat customer info is mapped to the local subscription snapshot from the `CleanSwipe Pro` entitlement; active entitlements unlock Pro, expired/missing entitlements fall back to Free.
3. A component calls `canUseFeature(featureKey)` from `useFeatureAccess`. Free features are always allowed; Pro features require `isProUser` (`subscriptionStatus === "active"` only).
4. On denial it calls `usePaywallStore.open(featureKey)`; the global `ProUpgradeSheet` shows a per-feature explanation and routes to the Premium tab.

Errors/loading states are mostly local and store-backed:
- Permission/loading/error state comes from `useAppStore`.
- Compression failures are normalized into friendly messages in `compression.store.ts`.
- Native service failures often return safe defaults, for example empty photo arrays or fallback estimates.
- Some errors are swallowed intentionally for prefetch, thumbnails, notifications, and external opening.

## 6. UI/UX Patterns
- Screens use React Native inline styles rather than StyleSheet-heavy or CSS-like styling.
- Theme values come from `useAppTheme`, which combines light/dark palettes with the selected accent color.
- Navigation is icon-forward, using `lucide-react-native` and `Pressable`.
- Common layout pattern: safe-area-aware headers, scroll views or flat lists, local cards/panels, and direct route pushes with `router.push`.
- Images should use `CachedImage` for `expo-image` caching, loading overlay, and consistent backgrounds.
- Videos should use `VideoMediaPlayer`, which wraps `expo-video` and centralizes autoplay, looping, muting, pause state, content fit, poster fallback behavior, and Android `textureView` rendering for overlay controls.
- Destructive actions require confirmation or an explicit review step. Marking for deletion is reversible; permanent deletion and original deletion are treated separately.
- Most visual surfaces use rounded cards/buttons, soft borders, and tabular number formatting for stats.
- Accessibility labels/roles are present on many buttons; continue adding them for icon-only actions.
- The app uses `boxShadow` style strings in several components; preserve existing style conventions when editing unless platform support becomes a problem.
- Brand mark: `AppLogo` (SVG) sits left-aligned with the wordmark in the app headers and follows the selected accent color. The launcher icon is an adaptive vector on a white background.
- Global overlays mounted in the ROOT layout (above the tab navigator) so they cover the tab bar: `ProUpgradeSheet` and the Smart Clean review sheet. Both are in-tree absolute overlays (NOT RN `<Modal>`) so safe-area insets read correctly; close via backdrop tap / Android back / Cancel.
- SUBSCRIPTION SAFETY (a real crash class this codebase hit): components must subscribe to primitives or STABLE refs only — never a selector that builds a fresh object/array (e.g. `Object.values`/`.map`) from store state. Derive such shapes in `useMemo`, expand persisted/compact data once on hydrate, and gate a feature with `canUseFeature(key)` — never an ad-hoc `isPro` check.
- Large media grids must be virtualized (`FlatList`), not mapped inside a `ScrollView` — a >5 GB review set is thousands of items.

## 7. Important Business Rules
- The app must have photo/video library permission before reading or deleting media.
- App-readable permissions are `granted` or `limited`; all other statuses should show permission recovery UI.
- Initial and refresh photo loads request the newest page using a page size of 80 so recent downloads/captures can appear quickly.
- Full-library discovery/estimation runs through `useMediaIndexStore.startFullScan` in pages of 60 and yields briefly between pages to reduce UI blocking.
- The app starts a full scan automatically only when no full scan has completed yet. After a saved estimate exists, refreshes should not repeatedly re-estimate.
- The media index is the source of truth for gallery assets. `useAppStore.photos` is kept empty for compatibility and should not be used for new UI.
- `PhotoAsset.monthKey` drives month grouping and filters.
- Visible swipe media excludes already reviewed IDs and items currently marked for deletion.
- A swipe scope with existing media but zero visible cards is considered complete, not empty.
- Starting a completed swipe scope over resets reviewed IDs only for the current month/media type.
- Start Over is blocked when the current scope still has marked-for-deletion items; the user must review/delete or restore those items first. This blocked state uses a custom in-app dialog, not the native Android alert.
- Next Month moves to the next older concrete month for the active media type. If there is no next month, the UI opens the month selector.
- Left swipe means delete/mark for deletion; right swipe means keep.
- Only the active swipe card should autoplay video. Background stack cards must remain static previews.
- Swipe-card videos loop automatically and are controlled only by mute/unmute from the card; pausing is reserved for the full preview/viewer.
- Full video previews should use in-app playback with loop and explicit play/pause controls.
- Marked items are deduplicated by `photoId`.
- Permanent deletion should only remove items from state after `PhotoLibraryService.deletePhotos` succeeds.
- Demo IDs starting with `demo-` are treated as deletable without native deletion, although demo data is not currently wired into app loading.
- Compression is allowed only for photos and videos.
- Compressible videos are those that are large by SIZE (≥ 8 MB estimated/known, `VIDEO_MIN_BYTES`) OR have a high enough bitrate (≥ 4 Mbps, `VIDEO_MIN_COMPRESSIBLE_BITRATE`). Bitrate = (bytes×8)/duration; the size check also serves as the fallback when duration/bitrate is unknown. (Updated 2026-06-15 — was bitrate-only ≥4 Mbps with a 20 MB unknown-bitrate fallback; the size floor was added so large lower-bitrate clips that still shrink are shown.)
- Compressible photos are those with at least 5 MB estimated/known size or at least 8,000,000 pixels.
- Cleanup filtering is based on indexed media, not on scroll-loaded media.
- Cleanup estimated savings are a single persisted global estimate for all indexed eligible media. The estimate is not split per photo/video filter.
- Cleanup exposes Estimate now/Estimate again to force a new full scan and replace the saved estimate.
- Cleanup Compress All is disabled until a saved full estimate exists, so it targets the full indexed cleanup opportunity rather than only the newest page.
- Compression qualities are `low`, `medium`, and `high`. The GLOBAL default is `settings.defaultCompressionQuality` (default `medium`), chosen in Settings → Compression; the per-item picker on the compression detail screen pre-selects it but can override it for a single compression.
- Compressed output must have a positive verifiable size before the original can be deleted.
- If compression saves no space, the UI should prefer keeping the original or deleting the compressed copy.
- Post-compression original policy can be `ask_every_time`, `keep_original`, or `delete_original_after_success`.
- Batch compression defers original-deletion decisions until the batch finishes.
- Deleting originals cannot be undone and must remain clearly marked as destructive.

Subscription / feature gating:
- `src/features/subscription/feature-flags.ts` is the SINGLE source of truth for which features are Free vs Pro. Move a feature between tiers there; nothing else changes.
- Pro is unlocked only by an active RevenueCat `CleanSwipe Pro` entitlement mapped to `subscriptionStatus === "active"`. The old `__DEV__` debug override and `EXPO_PUBLIC_FORCE_PRO` build unlock were removed and must not be reintroduced as entitlement paths.
- Gate every Pro feature with `canUseFeature(key)` and open the paywall on denial — no ad-hoc `isPro` checks in components.
- Ads render only for non-Pro users.

- RevenueCat offering/package convention: entitlement id `CleanSwipe Pro`, offering id `default`, packages/products mapped to `monthly` and `yearly`.
- RevenueCat API keys are build-time public env vars. For development-only Test Store flows, set `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_USE_TEST_STORE=1` plus `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_TEST_API_KEY`. For real store builds, leave the Test Store flag unset and use `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_ANDROID_API_KEY` / `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`.
- If RevenueCat keys or offerings are missing, Premium shows a configured error and disables purchase buttons instead of granting Pro or crashing.
- There is no custom backend for subscriptions; purchases, restores, and entitlement refreshes go through RevenueCat anonymous app users.

Smart Clean (Pro):
- Detectors run cheapest-first (`SMART_CLEAN_SCAN_ORDER`); cards display in `SMART_CLEAN_DETECTORS` order and each fills when its detector completes.
- Detection NEVER fabricates results; a missing native capability degrades a detector to "not available". Per-asset hashes are cached and invalidated by the asset's modification time.
- Keeper protection is enforced in THREE places (initial selection excludes keepers, toggle rejects keepers, confirm filters keepers). Smart Clean deletes ONLY under full (`granted`) access and ONLY via the single audited `permanentlyDeleteMarked` path.
- The scan keeps running while the app is backgrounded (foreground service) but cannot execute while the process is dead — it auto-resumes from its checkpoint on next launch. Manual Stop does NOT auto-resume; an interrupted (killed) scan does.

Advanced Stats (Pro):
- Cleanup events are emitted ONLY on terminal success paths; cancelled jobs never count. "Space reclaimed" counts actual deletions, not compression-only savings.

## 8. Development Commands
- Install dependencies: `npm install`
- Start Expo dev server: `npm run start`
- Start tunnel/dev-client flow: `npm run start:tunnel`
- Run on Android: `npm run android`
- Run on iOS: `npm run ios`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`
- i18n parity check: `npm run i18n:check` (validates all locales against `en.json`; exits non-zero on drift).
- Lint: `npm run lint` (ESLint 9 flat config; 0 errors expected, advisory warnings remain).
- Unit tests: `npm test` (Vitest; pure modules only — feature-access service, cleanup-report selectors, hash-utils).
- UI/E2E tests: `maestro test .maestro` (Maestro flows; needs the app installed + Metro running for a dev-client build, or a standalone release build, plus a Maestro install). See `.maestro/README.md`.
- Local Android debug APK after native dependency changes: set `JAVA_HOME` to Android Studio's bundled JBR if Java is not on PATH, then run `.\gradlew.bat :app:assembleDebug` from `android/` (Windows).
- macOS build env (this workspace since 2026-06-29): Android needs **JDK 17** — `JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@17/libexec/openjdk.jdk/Contents/Home` (the default JDK 25 is too new for Gradle 8.14) — plus `ANDROID_HOME=~/Library/Android/sdk` (adb lives at `$ANDROID_HOME/platform-tools/adb`, not on PATH) and `__UNSAFE_EXPO_HOME_DIRECTORY=/tmp/expo-home`. iOS: `npx expo run:ios` against the simulator (no signing needed); the FIRST build after a prebuild can fail on a ReactCodegen "Build input file cannot be found" race — just re-run. Vitest 4 needs Node ≥20.19 (`~/.nvm/versions/node/v22.15.0`).
- RevenueCat native integration changes require a native rebuild/reinstall; Metro-only reloads into an older APK will not include `react-native-purchases`.
- Standalone RELEASE APK (JS embedded, no Metro): `:app:assembleRelease` instead of `assembleDebug` (~2-4 min). The release buildType is debug-signed (template default) so `adb install -r` over the dev client KEEPS app data. There is no build-time Pro unlock; Pro requires an active RevenueCat subscription snapshot.
- Install local Android APK on a connected device: `adb install -r android/app/build/outputs/apk/{debug,release}/app-*.apk`.
- EAS Android preview/development builds: use `eas build --profile preview --platform android` or `eas build --profile development --platform android` if EAS CLI is installed and authenticated.

The npm scripts are `start`, `start:tunnel`, `start:demo` (Expo Go demo tunnel — see the 2026-06-11 demo entry), `android`, `ios`, `typecheck`, `i18n:check`, `lint`, and `test` — no `format` or `build`. Automated gates are `typecheck` + `i18n:check` + `lint` + `test` (added 2026-06-13).

## 9. Environment and Configuration
- No real `.env` file is committed; `.env.example` documents the expected keys and real `.env` / `.env.local` files are ignored. Recognized subscription env vars are `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_USE_TEST_STORE`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_TEST_API_KEY`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_ANDROID_API_KEY`, and `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`. Expo inlines `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` into the bundle. `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_USE_TEST_STORE=1` must be used only for RevenueCat Test Store development builds.
- `app.json` configures app name `SwipeClean` (display/launcher label; was "SwipeClean Free"), slug `swipeclean-free`, scheme `swipeclean`, Android package `com.cognitix.swipeclean`, iOS bundle ID `com.cognitix.swipeclean`, EAS project ID, media-library plugin, notifications plugin, video plugin, localization plugin, the `expo-secure-store` and `expo-local-authentication` plugins (the latter carries the iOS Face ID usage string / Android biometric permission — added 2026-06-11), and typed routes.
- Android permissions include image/video media reads, external storage compatibility, notifications, wake lock, foreground service, data sync foreground service, and Play Billing (`com.android.vending.BILLING`).
- `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` also currently includes media visual user selected, vibrate, internet, Play Billing, deep links, notification color metadata, and the `RNBackgroundActionsTask` foreground service. Unused main-manifest audio read, system overlay, write external storage, and legacy external storage mode were removed.
- `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/splashscreen_logo.xml` is a tracked vector fallback for the splash background. The generated PNG splash assets under density-specific drawable folders are ignored by the repo-wide `*.png` rule and should not be the only referenced splash logo in tracked Android XML.
- Launcher icon: an adaptive icon (`mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` + `ic_launcher_round.xml`) with a white background and the `drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml` vector mark overrides the legacy `mipmap-*/ic_launcher*.webp` rasters on API 26+. The display/launcher name is `SwipeClean` (`strings.xml` `app_name`).
- `eas.json` defines development, preview, and production profiles. Development and preview Android builds use APK output.
- `tsconfig.json` uses strict TypeScript and `@/*` path alias to `src/*`.
- `babel.config.js` includes the Reanimated plugin.
- `android/gradle.properties` enables Hermes, New Architecture, edge-to-edge display, GIF/WebP support, and the Expo dev-client network inspector.
- On this Windows workspace, local Gradle can use Android Studio's bundled JBR at `C:\Program Files\Android\Android Studio\jbr` when `JAVA_HOME` is otherwise missing.
- Native media/compression flows require a device or emulator with media assets and Android notification/media permissions.

## 10. Known Issues / Technical Debt

> **SCOPE (2026-08-01): this section is CURRENT-ONLY.** §12's "do not remove
> previous history" rule applies to **§11 Feature History**, not here — it had
> been applied to §10 as well, which left four items warning about problems that
> were already fixed (debug keystore, TEST rewarded ad unit, fail-open ads
> consent, R8 disabled). A stale known-issues list is worse than none: a fresh
> agent "fixes" what is already fixed and distrusts the rest of the file. Resolved
> items now move to the **Resolved** footer at the end of this section with the
> date and the commit/entry that closed them, instead of being edited in place or
> silently deleted.
- Lint (`npm run lint`, ESLint 9) and unit tests (`npm test`, Vitest) were added 2026-06-13; there is still no formatting script. Test coverage is currently only the pure modules (feature-access service, cleanup-report selectors, hash-utils) — store logic, screens, and services remain untested. The React-Compiler lint rules are set to "warn" and the codebase has ~95 advisory lint warnings not yet swept (mostly `react-hooks/refs|immutability|set-state-in-effect`, incl. Reanimated `.value` false-positives in the gesture screens).
- Still placeholders (UI/state only): feedback and bug report. RevenueCat billing is implemented client-side and verified end-to-end against the RevenueCat Test Store on device (a purchase unlocks Pro via the `CleanSwipe Pro` entitlement); real Play Store / App Store purchase + Restore verification still requires real store products and a store sandbox. Google AdMob is integrated and device-verified with TEST ads (banner + interstitial); a GDPR/UMP consent gather (`AdsConsentService`, now fail-CLOSED — see §10 Resolved) was wired before SDK init on 2026-06-13, but a real-ads release still needs the consent form/message configured in the AdMob console, a Play Data-Safety declaration, and an iOS AdMob app. (Language selection, subscription gating, Smart Clean, Advanced Stats, Account & Security, and reminder notifications are implemented; analytics/usage/error-reporting and the Privacy section were removed from Settings - see Feature History.)
- Smart Clean cold-cache cost: the FIRST scan after a data wipe rebuilds the media index (per-asset `getAssetInfoAsync`) AND recomputes every perceptual hash; the Tier-2 pixel detectors decode every photo. On a large library (~3,000 photos / 13 GB) this is minutes and CPU-bound. The feature cache makes subsequent scans fast. (The earlier "decode once for both dHash + blur" win is DONE — `computeGrayFeatures` decodes each photo once at the 64×64 grid and derives both, warmed by a concurrent pre-pass; the feature-cache version was bumped for the new dHash derivation.)
- The Smart Clean foreground-service scan survives backgrounding but NOT a killed process — it auto-resumes from its checkpoint on next launch. The compression↔scan RNBA hand-off depends on RNBA flipping `isRunning()` synchronously in `stop()`; verify on-device when both run together.
- Detector thresholds want tuning on real libraries: the screenshot detector over-matches some non-screenshots (Canva/WhatsApp/downloads); `DHASH_*`/`BLUR_VARIANCE_THRESHOLD` are untuned at scale. The meme detector is a conservative metadata heuristic (no on-device ML yet).
- Convert: new-format chips (MP3/WAV/M4A/WebM/GIF) surface only in a dev client that has the matching native module COMPILED IN — after touching `modules/*`, rebuild (`expo run:android` / `run:ios`), or the capability probes hide the chips. Runtime QA still pending (user does device testing): MP3 encode output (both platforms), WebM transcode output, iOS GIF (code-complete, build unconfirmed). Known engine limits: Android mp3/wav decode buffers the WHOLE PCM stream in memory (~10 MB/min stereo) — very long videos risk OOM (iOS streams instead); Android MP3 supports mono/stereo only; WebM v1 drops the audio track (VP8 video only, Opus later) and reports no progress; GIF caps at 15 s / 10 fps / 150 frames / 480 px. PARITY GAPS (blocked on vendoring approval): WebM has no iOS side (libvpx+libwebm), GIF has no Android side (no platform GIF encoder).
- Convert/LAME licensing + maintenance: LAME 3.100 is **LGPL v2** (COPYING vendored). Android links it as a separate `libswipecleanlame.so` (comfortable LGPL position); iOS statically compiles it into the pod — LGPL §6 relink-provision for a static App Store binary is a known gray zone to resolve before RELEASE (options: attribution + object-file provision, or move to a dynamic framework). The LAME tree is intentionally DUPLICATED (`lame/` for Android, byte-identical `ios/lame/` so the podspec has no `../` paths) — patches must be applied to BOTH copies; nothing enforces sync. `lame/config.h` is hand-written and load-bearing (see the module bullet in §3 for the ieee754/HAVE_NASM traps).
- Convert: conversion is non-destructive and has no "did it shrink?" check, so a format change may produce a larger file. The image (`expo-image-manipulator`) and video→MP4 (`react-native-compressor`) paths need no rebuild but still want broader real-device QA.
- Gallery multi-select gestures (2026-06-29 redesign): a single `Gesture.Exclusive(pan.activateAfterLongPress(180), tap)` over the FlashList — long-press-drag paints (deselect-by-anchor), tap toggles/opens, JS `setInterval` edge auto-scroll, a `painting` flag gates `scrollEnabled`; the right-edge date scrubber uses `onScrubbingChange`. NO overlay and NO `blocksExternalGesture`/`Gesture.Native` (both were tried and broke FlashList scroll — see Feature History). Month sections, the scrubber date bubble, normal scrolling, and the select-mode crash fix are device-verified; the full select-mode gesture feel (scroll-while-selecting, paint-deselect) and the single-month "Mon 24" label still want a final on-device pass.
- The launcher icon is a VECTOR recreation of the provided logo (a pasted chat image can't be written to disk as exact bytes); to ship the exact PNG, drop it in the repo and wire it as a raster.
- Compression and deletion are high-risk native flows. They need real-device testing across Android versions, permission states, limited access, large files, low storage, and interrupted foreground jobs.
- In-app video playback is wired with `expo-video`, but still needs real-device QA for local `expo-media-library` URIs, codecs, long videos, audio focus, and interaction with swipe gestures.
- PARTIALLY ADDRESSED (2026-06-13, needs a native rebuild to verify): **6K/8K video thumbnails.** Applied fix option (a) — `android:largeHeap="true"` (~512 MB heap) + raised `SAFE_MAX_DIMENSION`/`SAFE_THUMBNAIL_MAX_DIMENSION` 4096→8192, so frames up to 8K (~140 MB ARGB) now decode while a pathological >8K source still falls back to the placeholder. This change is in the committed `android/` manifest + JS but has NOT been rebuilt/verified on device — until a `:app:assembleDebug`/`assembleRelease` + reinstall, the running APK still uses the old behavior. Root cause recap: both expo-image/Glide's `VideoDecoder` and `react-native-compressor`'s `createVideoThumbnail` call the unscaled `MediaMetadataRetriever.getFrameAtTime`, so a ≥6K frame blew the old 256 MB heap (the chronic `MediaCodec.BufferInfo` OOM; see Feature History 2026-06-12). The resolution-INDEPENDENT fix (b) — a native downscaled extractor via `getScaledFrameAtTime` (API 27+) — remains deferred. Files: `src/components/media-thumbnail.tsx`, `src/services/compression-service.ts`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`.
- After adding or changing native dependencies, rebuild and reinstall the native Android binary. Hot reload/Metro updates into an older APK can crash with missing native module errors such as `Cannot find native module ExpoVideo`.
- The shared media index removes the old loaded-page limitation for Swipe, Cleanup, month selection, stats, previews, and largest-media screens after the full scan has completed and persisted.
- Full media indexing is cooperative JS/native async work, not a guaranteed OS-level background task. It yields between pages to reduce UI blocking, but very large/video-heavy libraries still need real-device performance testing.
- Newly downloaded/captured media should appear after the newest-page refresh on app launch, foreground, gallery-change event, or active-app polling. If the OS does not deliver a media-library event, it may wait until the next refresh interval.
- The persisted savings estimate can become stale after new media is added or compression decisions change. The user must tap Estimate again to refresh the global estimate.
- The media index can grow large because it persists metadata and estimates for the device library. Watch AsyncStorage size/performance on very large galleries.
- Size values may be estimated when native file size metadata is unavailable, so stats and savings can be approximate.
- RESOLVED (2026-06-13): the unused `src/constants/sample-photos.ts` and the unused components (`swipe-action-buttons`, `photo-list-item`, `review-delete-button`, `activity-calendar`) were deleted. The cheap `demo-`ID guards in the services were intentionally kept as defensive no-ops.
- Debug Android manifests still include `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` for development tooling. Do not copy that permission into release/main manifest without a clear feature need.
- Free video-compression unlock: the cap is 2/day, persisted in `swipeclean-free-video-quota` and reset at local midnight. (The "rewarded unit is a TEST placeholder" part of this item was resolved 2026-07-19 — see Resolved below.)
- **`EXPO_PUBLIC_ADS_USE_TEST` is an env-controlled release kill switch with no `__DEV__` guard** (`src/features/ads/ad-config.ts`) — structurally the same shape as the RevenueCat Test-Store flag that M1 was raised for, inverted: instead of shipping free Pro it ships $0 ads. Metro inlines `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` at BUILD time, so a bundle made with the flag set serves test ads permanently and cannot be corrected after the fact. `SwipeClean-closedtest-testads-v5.aab` (versionCode 5) IS such a build — verified by string-searching its `index.android.bundle`: the real unit IDs are absent, dead-code-eliminated. It must never be promoted. The 2026-08-17 build-6 artifact was cut separately with the flag removed and its embedded bundle was verified to carry all three real Android unit IDs with the forced-test warning branch absent. Mitigation added 2026-08-01: `assertAdConfigIsProductionSafe()` logs a loud startup warning in any release build carrying the flag, and the var is documented in `.env.example`. There is still no BUILD-time assertion, so every future release must repeat the embedded-bundle check.
- **A production-configured build-6 AAB now exists, but “technically uploadable” is not the same as “all release obligations complete.”** `dist-android/SwipeClean-v1.0.0-vc6-production.aab` is signed, production-configured, and verified (see `PLAY_RELEASE.md`). Public rollout still requires the Play Console declarations listed there and an explicit decision on the open LAME/LGPL attribution/source-offer risk below.
- Persisted-state migrations: 9 of the 10 zustand stores have no `version`/`migrate` (only `feature-cache-store.ts` does). Reshaping a persisted store silently corrupts installed users; the only guards today are ad-hoc `merge` backfills in `app-store.ts` (`normalizeSettings`) and the media index's `repairIndexedAssets`. Add `version` + `migrate` before changing any persisted shape.
- Offline entitlement never expires: `FeatureAccessService.isProUser` is a bare `subscriptionStatus === "active"`, and `expiresAt` is persisted (`subscription-store.ts`) but never compared to a date. A lapsed user who stays offline keeps Pro indefinitely, and an AsyncStorage edit on a rooted device grants it permanently. Deliberately NOT "fixed" so far because naive expiry revokes Pro from paying users in a billing grace period or a long offline stretch — any fix needs a grace window and a product decision, not just a date compare.
- **LGPL: LAME ships in the Android binary TWICE and CANNOT be removed by dropping our own copy.** `base/lib/*/libswipecleanlame.so` is our vendored LAME (built by `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/android/CMakeLists.txt`), and `base/lib/*/libandroidlame.so` comes from **react-native-compressor**, which declares `implementation 'com.github.kaushik-naik:TAndroidLame'` in its own `android/build.gradle` — react-native-compressor is load-bearing for the entire Compress feature, so that copy ships no matter what. Verified by `unzip -l` on the shipped v5 AAB. Hiding the MP3 target from `AUDIO_TARGETS` does not reduce the obligation (LGPL attaches to DISTRIBUTION, not UI exposure), and neither would dropping our vendored copy. **STATUS 2026-08-06: the Licenses screen that carried the attribution + written source offer was REMOVED on explicit instruction (see §11 entry 2026-08-06 (c)), so the app currently ships LGPL code with NO in-app attribution — SECURITY_SCAN H1 is fully open on both platforms.** Removing our vendored copy is still worth doing on its own merits (≈1.2 MB across 4 ABIs, ~150 vendored C files, and it would close the iOS static-link half of H1).
- 7 PRO feature keys are declared in `feature-flags.ts` but enforced nowhere (`compressAll`, `batchVideoCompression`, `smartClean`, `oneTapRecommendations`, `fasterScanning`, `cleanupHistory`, `compressionHistory`) — their UI was removed. `advancedCompressionSettings` is listed FREE, which makes the `openPaywall("advancedCompressionSettings")` branch in `compression-detail-screen.tsx` unreachable.
- `releaseForegroundService` (`smart-clean-store.ts`) has no caller, and the Smart Clean worker is now the ONLY importer of `react-native-background-actions` (there is no `background-compression-worker.ts` — compression runs inline). The documented "scan yields the FGS to compression" hand-off has no counterparty today; the `serviceHeld` bookkeeping is inert but harmless, and is worth keeping only if compression ever regains a foreground service.
- Smart Clean pre-pass memory: `image-pipeline.ts` obtains pixels via `expo-image-manipulator.manipulateAsync`, which decodes the FULL-resolution bitmap before downscaling — the exact behaviour `thumbnail-service.ts` documents as an OOM cause and deliberately avoids (it uses react-native-compressor's inSampleSize-during-decode instead). `pre-pass.ts` runs 4 of them concurrently, so up to 4 full-res ARGB bitmaps can be live at once. `android:largeHeap="true"` is currently the only thing absorbing it, and largeHeap is explicitly not the fix. A resolution-independent path (native downscale-during-decode, as the thumbnail service already has) is the real answer.
- `EXPO_PUBLIC_FORCE_PRO` was removed from `.env.example` and `start:demo` on 2026-06-13 after becoming inert in code. Older history entries mentioning FORCE_PRO or debug Pro unlocks are historical only and superseded by the active-subscription-only rule above. (The unused `compressionDetail.watchAdMessage` and the old subscription-display/privacy/proOverride i18n keys were pruned 2026-06-13.)
- The git worktree already had many modified/untracked files during this analysis. Future agents must avoid reverting unrelated user changes.

### RESOLVED (kept for traceability — do NOT re-open without re-checking the code)
- **Release signing / debug keystore** — RESOLVED 2026-07-13 (`7eeaac3`). `android/app/build.gradle` now throws a `GradleException` at task-graph-ready when `android/keystore.properties` is missing and a bundle/assemble/packageRelease task is in the graph; the debug-signing fallback is unreachable for release artifacts.
- **R8 disabled in release** (SECURITY_SCAN M9) — RESOLVED 2026-07-13. `minifyEnabled true` + `shrinkResources true` are unconditional on the release buildType. NOTE the `enableMinifyInReleaseBuilds` def in the same file is dead code — nothing reads it.
- **AdMob rewarded ad points at a TEST placeholder** — RESOLVED 2026-07-19 (`f506fc5`). Real rewarded units exist for both platforms (`ad-config.ts`); test IDs now serve only under `__DEV__` or `EXPO_PUBLIC_ADS_USE_TEST=1`.
- **iOS sample AdMob IDs / App ID** (SECURITY_SCAN M11) — RESOLVED 2026-07-19. Real iOS banner/interstitial/rewarded units and a real `iosAppId`; Google's sample `~1458002511` is gone.
- **Ads/GDPR consent fails OPEN** (SECURITY_SCAN H2, the top High finding) — RESOLVED 2026-07-13. `ads-consent-store.ts` defaults `canRequestAds:false`, `consent.service.ts` returns false on any UMP error, and interstitial/rewarded each check consent in both `preload()` and `maybeShow()`. §2 and §9 wording that still said "fail-open" was corrected 2026-08-01.
- **RevenueCat Test-Store flag ungated in release** (SECURITY_SCAN M1) — RESOLVED 2026-07-13. `revenuecat.service.ts` `getApiKey()` requires `__DEV__ &&` the env flag. Verified absent from the shipped v4/v5 bundles.
- **App Lock: plaintext PIN / no re-lock / no rate limit** (M3, M4, M5) — RESOLVED 2026-07-13. Salted SHA-256 with lazy migration, AppState re-lock after a 15 s grace, and a 4-try-then-exponential-backoff lockout. (Residual: the salt uses `Math.random()`, not a CSPRNG — documented in the file.)
- **7 of 9 "iOS BLOCKERS"** (§ iOS ↔ Android Parity Audit) — RESOLVED 2026-07-19 (`f506fc5`): app icon, three-way version mismatch, real ad units, Terms link, `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` removed, `UIBackgroundModes:audio` removed, `SKAdNetworkItems` added, `aps-environment` removed. STILL OPEN: the missing `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`, and `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` declaring zero collected data / no tracking while linking Google-Mobile-Ads-SDK (plus no `NSUserTrackingUsageDescription`).
- **16 KB native-library page alignment** — RESOLVED & VERIFIED 2026-07-16, re-verified 2026-08-01 against the shipped v5 AAB: all 26 `arm64-v8a` `.so` report LOAD `p_align 0x4000`.
- **Unbounded persisted deletion history** (SECURITY_SCAN L13) — RESOLVED 2026-08-01. `HISTORY_CAP = 2000` in `app-store.ts`, applied on write AND retro-trimmed on rehydrate.
- **Cancelled compression/conversion still wrote to the gallery** (SECURITY_SCAN M2, only half-fixed before) — RESOLVED 2026-08-01. Both job services re-check `isCancelled()` before the library write and delete the temp artifact instead.
- **`localCancelled` could suppress a legitimate entitlement** — RESOLVED 2026-08-01. `cancelSubscription()` used the local-cancel branch as the FALLBACK for any source that wasn't play_store/app_store, and `mapSource` collapsed PROMOTIONAL (a dashboard-granted complimentary entitlement) into `"none"` — so a user granted free Pro who tapped Cancel permanently suppressed their own grant client-side while the dashboard still showed it active. Fixed three ways: `SubscriptionSource` now carries `test_store` and `promotional` distinctly instead of collapsing them; the local-cancel branch requires `__DEV__ && source === "test_store"`; and the Settings row is hidden for non-cancellable sources. The persist `merge` also clears any stale `localCancelled` outside `__DEV__`, so anyone already stuck is repaired on next launch with no action.
- **NOTE on the v4 AAB verification method:** PROJECT_CONTEXT previously cited "`setForegroundServiceBehavior` present in `classes.dex`" as proof the RNBA patch compiled in. That check does not discriminate — the string is present in ALL FOUR AABs including v2/v3, which predate the patch, because `NotificationCompat.Builder` declares the method regardless. Do not reuse it as evidence.

## 11. Feature History
### 2026-09-07 - `add-app-ads-txt` merged into `main`; Gradle heap gotcha recorded, `.netlify` ignored
- **Merged to `main`:** the `add-app-ads-txt` branch, carrying the three 2026-09-05 entries below — the committed `website/` source + `app-ads.txt`, the shared `MediaPermissionGate`, and the `D:\DevCache` relocation + `-PswipecleanDevSxs` side-by-side debug flag. Fast-forward; no code changed in the merge itself.
- **`android/gradle.properties`:** documented, in the file itself, why `org.gradle.jvmargs` must stay at `-Xmx2048m`. Raising it to `4096m` on 2026-09-05 made native builds FAIL on this 16 GB machine — reserving more for the Gradle daemon starves the parallel `clang++` processes, which then die with *"The paging file is too small for this operation to complete. (0x5AF)"* during `:react-native-reanimated:buildCMake*`. The C++ toolchain, not Gradle, is the memory-hungry half of an Android build here. Also recorded: never pass `-Dorg.gradle.jvmargs` on the command line on Windows — the space inside the value breaks `gradlew.bat` when the repo path also contains one (`Codex On D`), and the build dies before it starts. Comments only; the value is unchanged.
- **`.gitignore`:** added `.netlify`, the Netlify CLI's local link/state folder. It caches the site's UI-configured build settings (`command = "expo export -p web"`, `publish = <repo>/dist`) that the 2026-09-05 website entry warns about; it is machine-local and must not be committed.
- Files: `.gitignore`, `android/gradle.properties`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification (run on the branch tip immediately before the merge): typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `npm run i18n:check` green (499 keys, 9 locales in parity with `en.json`, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings — exactly the documented baseline).
- Assumptions: none. The `MediaPermissionGate` work merged here is still **NOT DEVICE-VERIFIED** (see the 2026-09-05 (b) entry) — merging to `main` does not change that.
### 2026-09-05 (c) - All build caches moved off C: to D:\DevCache; side-by-side debug install flag
- **Why:** `C:` was down to **8.6 GB free** (228.9 GB used) while `D:` had ~500 GB. Three caches on `C:` accounted for ~29 GB: `~/.gradle` 11.69 GB, `~/.android` 9.92 GB (entirely AVD images), `%LOCALAPPDATA%\npm-cache` 7.76 GB. A native Android build on top of that is what a low-disk failure looks like.
- **Moved** (robocopy `/MOVE`, sources verified gone): `~/.gradle` → `D:\DevCache\gradle`; `%LOCALAPPDATA%\npm-cache` → `D:\DevCache\npm-cache`; `~/.android\avd` → `D:\DevCache\android\avd` (each AVD `.ini` rewritten, since they store absolute paths). Result: **C: 8.6 GB → 37.6 GB free.**
- **Pinned three ways** so it cannot silently regress: Windows **user** environment variables (`GRADLE_USER_HOME`, `ANDROID_AVD_HOME`, `ANDROID_EMULATOR_HOME`, `ANDROID_SDK_HOME`, `npm_config_cache`, `YARN_CACHE_FOLDER`) so plain terminals and Android Studio inherit it; **`.claude/settings.json`** (new) so every Claude Code session in this repo does, and which additionally pins `TMP`/`TEMP`/`TMPDIR` to `D:\DevCache\tmp` because Metro caches into `os.tmpdir()`; and an **`AGENTS.md` section** documenting the rule and the mapping table.
- **Deliberately NOT done:** system-wide `TEMP`/`TMP` were left untouched (repointing them globally is a known way to break installers; the project-scoped setting covers our builds). Only `~/.android\avd` was moved, not all of `~/.android` — the rest is ~10 KB of loose files including the **adb auth keys**, and relocating those forces an "Allow USB debugging" re-prompt on the connected device for zero space gain.
- **`android/app/build.gradle`:** the `debug` buildType gained an opt-in `-PswipecleanDevSxs=true` → `applicationIdSuffix '.devsxs'`, mirroring the release buildType's existing `-PswipecleanTabletQa`. Reason: the S24 carries the **Play-signed production build** (versionCode 7, installed 2026-08-23), and a locally-built debug APK is debug-keystore-signed, so `adb install -r` fails on signature mismatch — the only alternative being to uninstall the production app and its data. The suffix installs the dev client alongside it. A normal `assembleDebug` is unchanged. A debug build also keeps `__DEV__` true, so `ad-config.ts` serves Google TEST ads — a release build would serve REAL ads on the developer's own device, the top cause of AdMob account bans.
- Files: `.claude/settings.json` (new), `AGENTS.md`, `android/app/build.gradle`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: `gradlew --version` runs from the relocated `GRADLE_USER_HOME` without re-downloading the distribution; disk free re-measured after each move; AVD `.ini` rewrite confirmed. JS gates not re-run — no JS or TS changed.
### 2026-09-05 (b) - Fix the first-run media-permission dead end; shared MediaPermissionGate on every media screen
- **Root cause (the "asks once, then just blocks" bug).** `partialize` in `app-store.ts` deliberately refuses to persist a non-readable permission, rewriting it to `{ status: "not-requested" }`. That is correct for not exposing a stale index, but it **also discards `canAskAgain`**. So after a cold launch a permanently-denied user rehydrates as *never asked*; the gate computed `permanentlyDenied = status === "denied" && canAskAgain === false` → `false`, rendered **"Allow Access"**, and the tap called `requestMediaPermission()` — which the OS refuses to answer with a dialog (Android after two denials, iOS after one). **The button did nothing visible.** Nothing called `refreshPermissionStatus()` on gate mount, so the gate painted persisted state instead of live OS truth.
- **Second defect (the "granting in Settings isn't noticed" bug).** `usePhotoLibrarySync`'s poll + `MediaLibrary` listener are attached **only while access is readable** — the effect early-returns otherwise. In exactly the state the gate is shown there is no periodic safety net, leaving a single `AppState` "active" transition as the only recovery trigger.
- **Fix.** New `src/components/media-permission-gate.tsx` — one gate for every media screen. It calls `refreshPermissionStatus()` on mount **and on every `AppState` "active"**, so the CTA always reflects what the OS will actually do, and returning from system Settings is noticed even though the sync hook's listeners are detached. CTA re-fires the real OS dialog whenever `canAskAgain !== false` (so a once-denied user gets a genuine prompt, not a Settings deep-link) and routes to Settings only once the OS is finished asking. Its `AppState` listener is deliberately duplicated with the sync hook's: that one is about the library, this one is about escaping the gate, and it must keep working when the other is detached.
- Replaced three separately-maintained inline copies of the gate (`swipe-screen`, `history-screen`, `smart-clean-screen`) and **added one to `stats-screen`**, which previously rendered a screen of zeroes with no explanation. Settings and Premium are deliberately **not** gated — neither reads the library, and gating them would trap a denied user away from the app's own controls and the paywall.
- New i18n key `permissions.deniedMessage` across all 10 locales, replacing the hardcoded English string that `permission-service.ts` surfaced through `error`.
- **Not achievable, by OS design:** re-showing the system dialog "as the first time" once the user has exhausted the prompt. Android permanently stops presenting it after two denials, iOS after one. The gate therefore re-asks whenever the OS still permits and otherwise routes to Settings — which is the closest achievable behaviour.
- Files: `src/components/media-permission-gate.tsx` (new), `src/screens/swipe-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/history-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/smart-clean-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/stats-screen.tsx`, `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `i18n:check` green (499 keys, 10 locales in parity, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings — exactly the pre-change baseline). **NOT DEVICE-VERIFIED** — JS-only, so no native rebuild is needed, but the real first-run/deny/grant-from-Settings cycle must be walked on a device (fresh install, deny twice, tap the CTA, grant in Settings, return).
- Assumptions: none. The `canAskAgain` conflation hypothesis (Android's `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale` returning false both before the first ask and after permanent denial) was **investigated and ruled out** — `expo-modules-core`'s `PermissionsService.kt` guards it with its own `mAskedPermissionsCache`, computing `canAskAgain` only once status is already DENIED.
### 2026-09-05 - Website source committed to the repo; app-ads.txt added for AdMob verification
- **Trigger:** AdMob's "Add stores to app → Verify app" step failed with *"We didn't find an app-ads.txt file"*, reporting HTTP 404 for both `https://` and `http://effervescent-douhua-6f5c1d.netlify.app/app-ads.txt`. Confirmed independently by `curl` before changing anything. AdMob resolves the crawl target from the **developer website field of the Google Play listing**, which points at the Netlify privacy-policy host.
- **What changed:** new top-level `website/` directory holding the deployable site — `app-ads.txt` (the AdMob authorized-sellers record) and `index.html` (the privacy policy).
- `website/app-ads.txt` contains exactly one record, `google.com, pub-5256708773143000, DIRECT, f08c47fec0942fa0`, LF-terminated, no BOM and no CRLF (verified with `xxd`). The publisher id matches the app's real AdMob IDs in `app.json:66`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:30` and `src/features/ads/ad-config.ts` — so nothing on the app side was misconfigured; the file was simply never published.
- `website/index.html` is the **live privacy-policy page pulled down from Netlify** (`SwipeClean Privacy Policy`, effective 2026-07-07), with Netlify's serve-time injected `hosting-provider` / `netlify-deploy` meta block stripped. Content is otherwise byte-identical to what is deployed.
- **Why the whole site and not just `app-ads.txt`:** the site's content came from a **drag-and-drop deploy** (`Build from drop deployment`, 2026-07-07), it has **no git connection**, and a Netlify deploy **replaces the whole site**. Deploying a folder containing only `app-ads.txt` deletes everything else. That is not hypothetical — it is exactly what the first deploy of this change did (see the correction entry below).
- **The site ALSO has UI-configured build settings**, which the original analysis missed: `command = "expo export -p web"`, `publish = <repo>/dist`, both with `Origin = "ui"` (cached in the gitignored `.netlify/netlify.toml`). The command is broken (`react-native-web` is not installed) and `dist/` is **empty**. **Any "Trigger deploy" click in the Netlify dashboard therefore publishes an empty site**, 404-ing the privacy-policy URL that is baked into the shipped vc7 AAB (`src/config/contact.ts:24`) and referenced by the Play listing — a worse outcome than the AdMob warning. Deploy only with `--no-build` until those UI settings are cleared.
- This also closes the bus-factor gap recorded in `WEBSITE_BRIEF.md:197` ("No source file exists in the repo") — the deployed site is now versioned.
- **DEPLOYED 2026-09-05** to production (deploy `6a9c18d4a248090dd10a2cdd`) with `netlify deploy --prod --no-build --dir=website`. Live and verified: `/app-ads.txt` → `200 text/plain` with the exact record; `/` → `200` `<title>SwipeClean Privacy Policy</title>`; `/robots.txt` → `200`; the five security headers present. AdMob must still be asked to re-crawl from its console, and Google's crawl can take ~24 h.
- **Regression caused and fixed within the same session — read this before the next deploy.** The first production deploy (`6a9c147822ead7b2ff1b33a3`) shipped a folder containing only `index.html` + `app-ads.txt`, which **silently destroyed two files that the 2026-07-07 drop bundle had contained**: a `_headers` file (Content-Security-Policy, Permissions-Policy, Referrer-Policy, X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options — all five vanished, leaving only Netlify's default HSTS) and `robots.txt` (200 → 404). Neither file is visible over HTTP once deployed (`_headers` is consumed by Netlify at deploy time and always 404s), so **the deployed file list cannot be recovered by crawling the live site** — it was recovered by curling the previous deploy's permalink, `https://<deploy-id>--effervescent-douhua-6f5c1d.netlify.app`, and reading its response headers. Both files were reconstructed into `website/` and redeployed. **Rule: `website/` must contain the COMPLETE site, and any future deploy must be diffed against the previous deploy's permalink, not against the repo.**
- Files: `website/app-ads.txt`, `website/index.html`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `i18n:check` green (498 keys, 10 locales in parity, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). No source code changed — the gates are a no-regression baseline, not a test of this change.
- Assumptions: the Play listing's developer-website field still points at `effervescent-douhua-6f5c1d.netlify.app`. If it is later moved to the real domain planned in `WEBSITE_BRIEF.md`, `app-ads.txt` must be republished at the root of that domain too.
### 2026-08-22 (c) - LAME REMOVED ENTIRELY — no LGPL obligation left
- **Both copies of LAME are gone**, which retires the licence obligation rather than merely documenting it. Verified beforehand that nothing reachable used either copy:
  - **Ours (`libswipecleanlame.so`)** — called only by the `"mp3"` branch of `AudioEncodeModule`. MP3 was already absent from `AUDIO_TARGETS`, so no user could reach it. The other two targets never touched LAME: **m4a** is an AAC remux (MediaMuxer / AVAssetExportSession) and **wav** is MediaCodec/AVAssetReader PCM plus a hand-written RIFF header. Removing LAME therefore leaves m4a and wav fully intact.
  - **react-native-compressor's (`libandroidlame.so`)** — backs its AUDIO compression API. This app imports only `Image`, `Video`, `createVideoThumbnail` and `getFileSize`, with zero `Audio.compress` call sites, so it was dead weight that shipped purely because the dependency declares it.
- Deleted: `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/lame/` and the byte-duplicated `ios/lame/` (132 files, ~2.4 MB of vendored C), the JNI bridge `src/main/cpp/lame_jni.c`, `ios/SCLameEncoder.{h,m}`, and the module's `CMakeLists.txt`. The module's `android/build.gradle` loses its `externalNativeBuild`/CMake block entirely — there is no native source left to compile — and the podspec drops `HAVE_CONFIG_H`, the LAME header search paths, `public_header_files`, `preserve_paths` and the LAME-only warning suppression, leaving `source_files = "*.swift"`.
- Removed from the API surface: `supportsMp3()` (Kotlin + Swift), `LameBridge` (both platforms), the `"mp3"` encode branches, `ConvertCapabilities.audioMp3`, the `mp3` member of `ConvertAudioTarget`, its OUTPUT_KIND/mime/label map entries, the `isTargetAvailable` case, the `audio-mp3-unavailable` error code, and `convert.note.mp3` across all 10 locales.
- **The dependency's copy is excluded in Gradle**, not deleted — `configurations.configureEach { exclude group: "com.github.kaushik-naik", module: "TAndroidLame" }` in `android/app/build.gradle`. Kotlin resolves classes lazily, so this is safe while `AudioCompressor` is never instantiated. **If anything ever starts using react-native-compressor's audio compression, this exclusion must be removed** — the symptom is a `NoClassDefFoundError` at the first audio-compress call, and the LGPL obligation returns with it.
- Consequence: the app no longer distributes LGPL code, so the removed Open-Source Licenses screen (2026-08-06 c) is no longer a compliance gap. SECURITY_SCAN **H1** (LAME/LGPL, both the Android attribution half and the iOS static-link half) is closed by removal rather than by attribution. Expected size saving: roughly 1.2 MB x 4 ABIs from our copy plus ~0.9 MB x 4 from the dependency's.
- Re-adding MP3 later is now a real decision, not a one-line un-hide: it means re-vendoring an LGPL encoder and taking the attribution + source-offer obligations back on. The comment in `convert-targets.ts` says so, replacing the older "re-add mp3 here to bring the chip back" note which is no longer true.
- Files: `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/**` (deletions + `android/build.gradle`, `ios/SwipeCleanAudioEncode.podspec`, `AudioEncodeModule.kt`, `AudioEncodeModule.swift`), `android/app/build.gradle`, `src/features/convert/convert-targets.ts` + test, `convert.types.ts`, `convert.store.ts`, `engine/audio-engine.ts`, `engine/conversion-engine.ts`, `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `i18n:check` green (0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). **NOT BUILD-VERIFIED — deliberately.** The user asked to hold the versionCode bump and the build until more changes land, so the Kotlin/Swift/podspec/Gradle edits have only been checked at source level. The next Android build is the first real test of the CMake removal and the TAndroidLame exclusion; confirm afterwards with `unzip -l` that NO `*lame*.so` remains in the artifact.
- versionCode deliberately left at 7. The next Play upload still needs 8 or higher.

Deleting our copy would NOT remove the obligation — the dependency's copy ships regardless, and dropping react-native-compressor would mean losing Compress. Google Play does not scan for this, so nothing will auto-flag it; the exposure is a rights-holder/enforcement complaint and a possible takedown request — low probability, non-zero, and permanent while shipping. ### 2026-08-22 - Production AAB build 7 (versionCode 7) cut for Google Play review
- Built `:app:bundleRelease` → `dist-android/SwipeClean-v1.0.0-vc7-production.aab` (83.3 MB), SHA-256 `381CE9F2C15150D4B6B81593575234777C98C9B5288A61612361878AB74DE1FA`. Full verification record in `PLAY_RELEASE.md`.

The contents differ from 6 regardless (green rebrand, new ring logo, swipe "new media" pill, delete progress overlay, themed delete dialogs, media-sync fix, licenses screen removed). versionCodes need not be contiguous, so bumping is always the safe call.
- Verified before handoff, each against the artifact rather than the source: `jarsigner -verify` → "jar verified" with the `CN=Cognitix` upload key (SHA-256 `B9:0F:F3:…:8D:1F`); versionCode read out of the AAB's protobuf manifest as 7 and **cross-checked differentially against the vc6 artifact, which reads 6** (the protobuf stores the value as an ASCII string, so a single-file read is easy to misparse — the differential is what makes it trustworthy); embedded JS contains all three PRODUCTION AdMob unit IDs, confirming this is not a forced-test-ads build; this cycle's features present and the removed surfaces still absent.
- Two submission caveats recorded in `PLAY_RELEASE.md` rather than left implicit: the **Play listing icon uploads separately from the AAB**, so the store entry keeps the old blue card-stack mark until the new green ring PNG is uploaded in Play Console; and **LGPL attribution is absent by explicit product decision** while LAME still ships twice in the binary — a higher risk bar at production review than at closed testing.

### 2026-08-21 (d) - Launcher icon resized to fit the safe zone; green applied to EXISTING installs via a real migration
- **Launcher icon was overflowing the adaptive-icon safe zone — arithmetic error in entry (c).** That entry justified `scaleX/Y = 0.82` by claiming the art spans ~35 of 48 viewport units. Wrong: the ring's stroke adds to its radius, so the true outer radius is `17.5 + 5.4/2 = 20.2` and the art spans **40.4** units. At 0.82 that is `40.4 x 0.82 = 33.1` units = 69% of the icon = **74.5dp, over the 72dp safe zone** — so the launcher's mask could clip it, which is exactly what the user saw ("too big"). Corrected to **0.62** → 25.0 units = 56.4dp, comfortably inside, and visually balanced against other launcher icons. The derivation is now a comment in the vector so the next change re-checks it.
- **Store PNGs re-rendered with matching padding.** The 1024/512 rasters previously used `viewBox="0 0 48 48"`, so the mark filled 84% of the canvas — much tighter than the (now smaller) launcher icon, making the two read as different logos. Both now render at `viewBox="-6 -6 60 60"` (~67% fill). `assets/logo-mark.svg` updated to the same viewBox so re-renders stay consistent.
- **Green now actually applies to existing installs.** Entry (b) changed `defaultSettings.accentColor` to green, but `normalizeSettings` backfills only MISSING settings and never overrides an existing value — so every existing install kept the old `"blue"` default and the rebrand was invisible on-device. Added the **first versioned migration on `useAppStore`**: `version: 1` plus a `migrate` that flips a persisted `accentColor === "blue"` to `"green"` exactly once.
  - zustand treats a blob with no `version` as 0, so this runs once per existing install and never again.
  - CORRECTED TWICE, both worth knowing. (1) First written as `version: 1` scoped to the old default `"blue"` only, to preserve deliberate choices — a no-op on the test device, whose accent had been switched to pink, so the rebrand stayed invisible. Broadened to re-point EVERY existing install at green: green is the brand, not merely the new default. (2) Broadening the v1 rule STILL did nothing, because the device had already recorded version 1 from the previous build and **zustand only runs `migrate` when the STORED version is lower than the code's**. Re-pointing an already-migrated install requires a NEW version number, never an edit to the old rule — so it shipped as `version: 2`. Verified green on device after the v2 build. The reasoning is duplicated as a comment in `app-store.ts` so the next migration does not repeat it.
  - This also closes part of the §10 "9 of 10 stores have no version/migrate" debt for the largest store — the pattern to copy for the others.
- Files: `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml`, `src/store/app-store.ts`, `assets/icon.png`, `assets/logo-mark.svg`, `ios/SwipeClean/Images.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/App-Icon-1024x1024@1x.png`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). The 1024 PNG was visually inspected after re-render. The safe-zone fit still wants a real launcher check — clipping only manifests on-device.
- RULE for anyone changing this mark: the adaptive-icon safe zone is the inner 72dp of 108dp (66.7%). Compute the art span from the STROKE-INCLUSIVE bounds, not the path radius, then pick a scale that keeps it under that. Getting this wrong is silent — it only shows as a clipped icon on a device.

### 2026-08-21 (c) - New brand mark: green "space-freed ring" replaces the card-stack logo
- **New logo, new SHAPE — not a recolour.** The old mark (tilted card stack + three swipe-motion arcs) is replaced by an open storage ring around a photo glyph: a meter with a gap at the top-left, reading as "space reclaimed" — the product's actual promise rather than the gesture that delivers it. Chosen by the user from six shape concepts.
- **Why the old mark had to change beyond colour:** its three motion arcs sat at 0.5 / 0.3 / 0.16 opacity and the whole foreground was scaled to 0.44 inside the adaptive icon, so at real launcher size (~40-48dp) the arcs were effectively invisible and the mark collapsed to an ambiguous blob. The ring is a single 5.4-width stroke and stays legible when shrunk.
- **One geometry, four outputs.** The same paths drive all of them — change one, re-render the rest:
  - `src/components/app-logo.tsx` — in-app SVG (`AppLogo`), used by the header and all three App-Lock states.
  - `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml` — adaptive-icon foreground. `<circle>` is not valid in an Android vector, so the sun is expressed as a two-arc path. Group scale is **0.82**: the art spans ~35 of 48 viewport units, and the adaptive-icon safe zone is the inner 72dp of 108dp (66.7%), so 35 x 0.82 ≈ 28.7 units ≈ 64.6dp fits inside it. Raising this scale will get the ring clipped by the launcher's circular mask.
  - `assets/icon.png` + `ios/SwipeClean/Images.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/App-Icon-1024x1024@1x.png` — 1024x1024 raster, rendered from the SVG with headless Chrome (`--headless=new --screenshot --window-size=1024,1024`). Deliberately OPAQUE white background: the App Store rejects icons with an alpha channel.
  - `assets/logo-mark.svg` — NEW, the master vector. Kept in-repo so the PNGs can be re-rendered without reconstructing the geometry from the component.
- **Accent-following, with a fixed exception.** `AppLogo` is two-tone (ring + darker glyph) and every call site passes the selected `theme.accent`, so hard-coding a green glyph would have broken the mark for the other four accents. The glyph colour is now derived via a local `shade(color, 0.72)`. The launcher and store icons stay FIXED green on purpose — a home-screen icon must not change when the user picks a different in-app accent.
- Store assets for the listing were exported at 1024x1024 (App Store / `assets/icon.png`) and 512x512 (Play Store listing icon) and handed to the user directly.
- Files: `src/components/app-logo.tsx`, `android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml`, `assets/icon.png`, `assets/logo-mark.svg` (new), `ios/SwipeClean/Images.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/App-Icon-1024x1024@1x.png`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). Both PNGs were visually inspected after rendering (a first pass had the sun glyph merging into the mountain slope and was re-cut). NOT device-verified — the adaptive-icon safe-zone scale in particular wants a real launcher check, since clipping only shows on-device.
- NOTE: `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` still uses `@android:color/white` as the background layer, which is correct for this mark (green on white). If a future concept becomes a full-bleed tile, the colour must move into `<background>` — a full-bleed FOREGROUND gets clipped by the launcher mask.

### 2026-08-21 (b) - Delete flow: in-app dialogs + deleting progress; green is the default accent
- **Native `Alert.alert` replaced with the themed `AppDialog`** in `review-delete-list-screen.tsx`. Both the completion and the failure popups were raw OS alerts in an otherwise fully themed flow. The failure one is the path the user hit: denying the OS delete-consent dialog makes `PhotoLibraryService.deletePhotos` treat `deleteAssetsAsync === false` as a failure and throw, which surfaced an OS-styled alert on cancel. `AppDialog`'s own doc comment already said it exists to replace `Alert.alert` — this screen simply was never migrated.
  - Success/error are now expressed via `tone`, and the capped post-delete interstitial moved from the Alert button's `onPress` to the dialog's `onDismiss`, so the ad still fires at the same natural task-end.
- **Deleting progress screen.** `confirmDelete` awaited the whole batch with nothing on screen, so a large marked queue looked frozen and invited repeat taps. A full-bleed foreground overlay (spinner + "Deleting…" + explanatory line) now covers the list while the native delete runs, and clears in a `finally` so it can never strand on an error path. The user stays on — and returns to — the marked-for-deletion screen, as requested.
- **Green is now the default accent.** `defaultSettings.accentColor` blue → green, and `accentColors` reordered so green leads the Settings → Appearance swatches. IMPORTANT BEHAVIOURAL NOTE: this only affects installs with NO persisted accent. `normalizeSettings` backfills missing settings but never overrides an existing value, so any device that already has `accentColor: "blue"` persisted keeps blue until the user picks green (or clears data). That is deliberate — silently overwriting a user's chosen accent would be worse — but it means the change is invisible on an existing install.

### 2026-08-21 - Swipe deck surfaces newly arrived media ("N new" pill); manage-subscription row restored
- **Manage subscription restored.** The Settings → Support row is the app's ONLY route to the store's subscription-management page (Play on Android, Apple on iOS). Entry 2026-08-01 (d) hid it behind `isCancellableSource` (play_store/app_store/dev-test_store), which for any other `source` removed the manage entry point entirely — the user lost the ability to reach their own subscription. The row is now shown for EVERY `subscriptionStatus === "active"` user again.
  - The promo-grant self-revoke bug this gate was guarding against stays fixed, because the fix that actually matters lives in `cancelSubscription` itself: local revocation still requires `__DEV__ && source === "test_store"`. Hiding the row was belt-and-braces that cost a feature.
  - `cancelSubscription` restructured so **opening the store page is the DEFAULT** and local-cancel is the narrow special case. Previously the non-store branch was a no-op; now every non-test-store source calls `openManageSubscriptions`, which already falls back to the platform's generic subscriptions URL when RevenueCat supplied no `managementUrl` — so there is always a valid destination and entitlement is never revoked locally.
- **Swipe deck missed newly downloaded media — root-caused.** Reported as "a new download shows in the gallery immediately, but on the swipe screen I have to open and close the app 2-3 times". Not a detection failure and NOT the same bug as 2026-08-06 (b):
  - `refreshPhotos` (`app-store.ts`) deliberately RE-ANCHORS `currentIndex` to the photo you were on: it finds `currentPhotoId` in the rebuilt `visiblePhotos` and moves the index to match. That correctly preserves your place.
  - But the deck is newest-first, so a newly arrived asset is PREPENDED at index 0 — i.e. BEHIND the anchored position. The deck only ever moves forward (swiped cards are filtered out of `visiblePhotos`), so index 0 is never revisited.
  - `currentIndex` is NOT persisted (absent from `partialize`), so it resets to 0 on relaunch — which is exactly why restarting the app surfaced the item and made this look intermittent.
- Fix (per product decision: preserve position, do not auto-jump): new `useAppStore.jumpToNewestMedia()` sets `currentIndex: 0`, and the Swipe screen renders an accent "N new items" pill when anything sits ahead of the current card. `newAheadCount` is simply `activeIndex` — since swiped cards leave `visiblePhotos`, anything still in front of you can only have been inserted after you passed that position.
- **`check-i18n.mjs` is now plural-aware.** i18next resolves `t("a.b", { count })` to `a.b_one` / `a.b_other` at RUNTIME, so source only ever contains the BASE key and the orphan detector reported every correctly-pluralised string as dead. `findOrphanKeys` now strips CLDR suffixes (`_zero/_one/_two/_few/_many/_other`) before matching. Without this, adding any plural string would have pressured the next agent into deleting a live key.
- Files: `src/store/subscription-store.ts`, `src/screens/settings-screen.tsx`, `src/store/app-store.ts`, `src/screens/swipe-screen.tsx`, `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `scripts/check-i18n.mjs`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (14 files / 123 tests); `i18n:check` green (0 orphans, plural keys correctly recognised); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). NOT device-verified — the pill needs an on-device check with a real download while mid-deck.
- Assumption: `activeIndex` is a sound proxy for "new arrivals ahead". Undo restores a prior index and month/type switches reset it to 0, so the only way to sit at index > 0 with items in front is insertion ahead of you. If a future change lets the deck seek backwards, this derivation needs revisiting.

### 2026-08-17 (d) - External gallery deletions prune ghost media; tablet QA and release artifacts refreshed
- What changed: fixed externally deleted photos/videos remaining as ghost tiles. Root cause: the 3-second signature probe correctly detected count changes, but full-access `refreshPhotos` only ran the merge-only newest-page refresh, while the later full scan skipped once an earlier full scan was marked complete; nothing ever removed missing IDs. Full-access lifecycle/library reconciles now take a paginated ID-only MediaStore/PhotoKit snapshot, verify its count and newest-item signature stayed stable from first page to final probe, and abort without pruning on any permission/read/pagination/concurrent-change uncertainty. IDs proven absent are removed from the media index, reviewed IDs, marked-deletion queue, stale last-swipe state, and Smart Clean feature cache; current position is clamped, Smart Clean snapshots are invalidated/rescanned when applicable, and external deletions deliberately do not increment cleanup history/stats. Limited access keeps its existing authoritative pruning full scan because absence there can mean permission scope rather than deletion. The reconciliation runs on launch, foreground return, native library events, the safety poll, and any changed cheap signature, so it also repairs deletions made while SwipeClean was closed. Added focused tests for missing-ID selection and stable/unstable/incomplete native snapshots. Added an opt-in `-PswipecleanTabletQa=true` release suffix (`com.cognitix.swipeclean.tabletqa`) so a Play-signed installation can remain intact while an upload-key build runs side-by-side; normal APK/AAB application IDs are unchanged. Refreshed the build-6 production AAB/APK after the fix and preserved the installed QA APK separately.

  - `libswipecleanlame.so` — our vendored LAME 3.100, built by `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/android/CMakeLists.txt`.
