### 2026-08-06 (b) - GOTCHA: `creationTime` is DATE_TAKEN — downloads have none, so the newest-page fetch could never see them
- **Symptom:** downloading an image/video showed "Finding media…" in the app but the file never appeared, while the system gallery showed it instantly. The 3 s probe from entry (a) WAS working — detection fired and a refresh ran. The refresh itself was structurally incapable of returning the new asset.
- **Root cause — verified on a real device, not inferred.** `expo-media-library`'s `creationTime` maps to `MediaStore.Images.Media.DATE_TAKEN` (`MediaLibraryEnums.kt` / `AssetUtils.kt`), which is an **EXIF-derived field that only CAMERAS set**. Querying the device MediaStore directly (`content query --uri content://media/external/file --sort 'date_added DESC'`) showed every recently downloaded file with `datetaken=NULL` and only a device screenshot carrying a real value:
  - `0ac3655ced73986f908eee42d358eb9f.jpg` → `datetaken=NULL` (download)
  - `SwipeClean-Promo-1080x1920-1.mp4` → `datetaken=NULL` (download)
  - `Screenshot_20260806_131446_One UI Home.jpg` → `datetaken=1786014886846` (capture)
  The quick refresh fetched `first: 80` sorted by `creationTime` DESC, so a `DATE_TAKEN=NULL` asset sorted to the very END of a 3,658-asset library — unreachable by a first-page fetch. Only a FULL scan could ever index a download, which is why one appeared after a fresh install (full scan runs) but never afterwards.
- **Fix:** `refreshNewestPage` now issues TWO queries in parallel and merges them (deduped by id): newest by `creationTime` (DATE_TAKEN — camera captures) AND newest by `modificationTime` (DATE_MODIFIED — downloads, shares, transfers, edits; the filesystem always sets it). `getPhotosPage` takes a `sortBy` option to support this. `getLibrarySignature` also switched to `modificationTime`, so a download changes `newestId` instead of being invisible to the fingerprint (previously only `totalCount` moved). Pagination still follows the `creationTime` cursor, so loadMore/full-scan semantics are unchanged, and the index's own `resolveMediaDate` (creationTime ?? modificationTime) places a download at the TOP by its modification date rather than at the bottom as undated.
- **Rule for future work: never treat `creationTime` as "when this arrived on the device".** It is "when the photo was taken" and is absent for anything not produced by the camera. Use `modificationTime` for recency-of-arrival, or query both.
- Also confirmed NOT a bug, after a false start: the support email showing a recipient named "Tes Test". A **bare** `mailto:info.cognitix@gmail.com` fired from adb with no subject/body and no app code involved reproduces it, and the device contacts provider contains no matching entry — so Gmail is resolving the address to its **Google account profile name** server-side. The app's recipient is correct (the generated URI parses to exactly `info.cognitix@gmail.com`); changing the display requires renaming that Google account, not an app change. Do not "fix" this in code.
- Files: `src/services/photo-library-service.ts`, `src/store/media-index-store.ts`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (10 files / 109 tests); `i18n:check` green (500 keys, 0 orphans). Device-verified root cause via direct MediaStore query; the FIX itself still needs an on-device check (download a file, confirm it appears within a few seconds).

### 2026-08-06 - New media appears within ~3 s (was up to 45 s); contact/legal constants centralized
- **New-media latency — root-caused, then fixed cross-platform (no native code).** Newly downloaded/captured media took up to 45 s to appear, while the system gallery showed it instantly. TWO compounding causes, both verified in source rather than assumed:
  1. **`MediaLibrary.addListener` is not a reliable instant signal.** In the installed expo-media-library 18.2.1, the Android `MediaStoreContentObserver.onChange` re-queries the total asset count and emits the JS event ONLY when that count differs from the last observed value (`MediaLibraryModule.kt`). So an add+delete inside one window, a staged/pending MediaStore insert, or a count query racing the insert swallows the event entirely. (The observers themselves ARE registered correctly, on `MediaStore.Images/Video.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI` with `notifyForDescendants=true` — the registration was never the problem.) Expo's own docs confirm the Android event carries no detail: "On Android it's invoked with an empty object", `hasIncrementalChanges` always false.
  2. **The fallback poll was the real latency floor.** It ran every 45 s and called the FULL reconcile — a permission read plus `refreshNewestPage`, which maps every asset through `mapAsset` → `getAssetInfoAsync` PER ASSET (80 native round-trips for an 80-asset page). That expense is precisely why the interval was set so slow.
- Fix: **separate DETECTION from RECONCILIATION.** New `PhotoLibraryService.getLibrarySignature()` does ONE `getAssetsAsync({ first: 1 })` and reads `totalCount` off the same result — no per-asset info calls — returning `{ totalCount, newestId, newestModificationTime }`. `usePhotoLibrarySync` runs it every **3 s while foregrounded** and escalates to the expensive reconcile only when the pure `librarySignatureChanged()` says the library actually changed. The 45 s full poll stays as a safety net for scope/permission drift, and `addListener` stays wired (free, and the fastest signal when it does fire).
- The three fingerprint fields are each load-bearing: `totalCount` catches adds and deletes; `newestId` catches a **same-count swap** (one added, one removed — the exact case the Android native observer misses); `newestModificationTime` catches an in-place edit (crop/markup) that changes neither. A missing baseline returns `false` so the first probe after attaching only seeds, never fires a redundant refresh on top of the launch reconcile.
- **Platform-neutral by construction** — one query shape, only fields expo-media-library returns on both platforms, zero `Platform.OS` branches. Probing is skipped whenever `AppState.currentState !== "active"` (nothing is visible in the background, and the existing AppState listener already forces a refresh on return), and the cached fingerprint is reset when read access is lost so a scope change can't be compared against a library from a different scope.
- **Contact/legal constants centralized** into new `src/config/contact.ts` (`SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL`, `TERMS_OF_USE_URL`). They were duplicated — `SUPPORT_EMAIL` in both the settings and licenses screens, `TERMS_OF_USE_URL` in both the premium screen and the upgrade sheet — with `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL` exported from a SCREEN that two other modules imported. Changing the support address meant editing several files; the LGPL source-request mail in the licenses screen is the easy one to miss, and a missed one silently sends user mail to a dead address. Verified the support address resolves correctly: the generated URI parses to recipient `info.cognitix@gmail.com` exactly. (A mail client showing a saved CONTACT NAME instead of the raw address is client display behaviour, not a wrong recipient.)
- Files: `src/utils/library-signature.ts` (new), `src/utils/library-signature.test.ts` (new, 7 cases), `src/services/photo-library-service.ts`, `src/hooks/use-photo-library-sync.ts`, `src/config/contact.ts` (new), `src/screens/settings-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/licenses-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/premium-screen.tsx`, `src/components/pro-upgrade-sheet.tsx`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (**10 files / 109 tests**, +7); `i18n:check` green (500 keys, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). NOT device-verified — the 3 s cadence and the actual perceived latency want an on-device check with a real download, on both platforms.
- **VERIFIED (not assumed): `first: 1` does NOT truncate `totalCount`, on either platform.** Android's `getAssets` passes NO limit to `contentResolver.query(...)` — `limit`/`offset` are applied afterwards inside `putAssetsInfo`, which only materializes that many rows, so `assetsCursor.count` is the full accessible result-set count (`assets/GetAssets.kt`). iOS reads `fetchResult.count` off the whole `PHFetchResult` and paginates by index slicing afterwards (`MediaLibraryUtilities.swift`). Both also narrow correctly under restricted access by construction: Android 14 partial access makes the MediaStore query return only user-selected rows, and iOS limited authorization makes `PHAsset.fetchAssets` return only selected assets — so the count tracks the ACCESSIBLE set, which is what the fingerprint wants. No "selected photos" over/under-firing.
- Assumption still open: a 3 s single-asset query is cheap enough to run continuously while foregrounded. It is far cheaper than the 80 `getAssetInfoAsync` calls it replaces, but it IS still a real MediaStore/PhotoKit query per tick — if on-device battery/CPU measurement says otherwise, raise `CHANGE_PROBE_INTERVAL_MS`; it is a single constant.

### 2026-08-01 (d) - Promo grants can no longer be self-revoked (`localCancelled`); indexing pill floats instead of shifting layout
- What changed:
  - **`localCancelled` can no longer touch a production entitlement.** The local-cancel path is a RevenueCat **Test Store** workaround (a test subscription cannot be cancelled by the client SDK, and there is no store page to open), but it was written as the FALLBACK for any source that wasn't play_store/app_store. Since `mapSource` collapsed everything else into `"none"`, a **PROMOTIONAL** (dashboard-granted, complimentary) entitlement landed in it — and such a grant also has no `managementURL`. A user given free Pro who tapped "Cancel Subscription" wrote a persisted `localCancelled: true`, after which `reconcileEntitlement` suppressed the grant on every refresh and every customer-info push, forever, while the RevenueCat dashboard still showed it active and re-granting changed nothing. (Recoverable only via "Restore Purchases", which also clears the flag — not a discoverable undo for "Cancel".)
  - Three-part fix: (1) `SubscriptionSource` now carries **`test_store`** and **`promotional`** as distinct values instead of collapsing them into `none` — the SDK's `Store` union has 12 members and `TEST_STORE`/`PROMOTIONAL` are separate, so the code can identify the Test Store POSITIVELY rather than inferring it from absence; (2) the local-cancel branch requires `__DEV__ && source === "test_store"` — the Test Store is already unreachable outside `__DEV__` (see `getApiKey`), so the workaround for it has no business existing in a release build; (3) the Settings "Cancel Subscription" row is hidden unless the source is actually cancellable, since a Cancel button on a complimentary grant cannot do what it says.
  - **Self-heal for anyone already affected:** the persist `merge` now forces `localCancelled: false` outside `__DEV__`. The flag can no longer be SET in a release build, so any `true` there is a leftover from the old behaviour — cleared on next launch, no user action, no support round-trip. Dev builds keep their flag (behaviour intentionally unchanged, per the product call: "on a dev, we could stay like this").
  - Verified as a branch matrix rather than by inspection — prod: every source is either "open the store page" or a no-op that preserves entitlement, and NOTHING reaches local-cancel; dev: only `test_store` does. Stale `true` → repaired in prod, preserved in dev.
  - **Indexing pill is now a floating notification.** `IndexingIndicator` was an in-flow element, so the swipe card (and the gallery grid) visibly jumped down when indexing started and back up when it finished — on a large library that is most launches. It is now absolutely positioned with `pointerEvents="none"`, mounted as the last child of the content container so it paints above the media, with a drop shadow and a drop-in/lift-out animation (`FadeInDown`/`FadeOutUp`) so it reads as a layer above the page rather than a band within it. Takes zero layout space; the swipe deck stays fully interactive underneath.
- Files: `src/features/subscription/subscription.types.ts`, `src/features/subscription/revenuecat.service.ts`, `src/store/subscription-store.ts`, `src/screens/settings-screen.tsx`, `src/components/indexing-indicator.tsx`, `src/screens/swipe-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/selected-photos-screen.tsx`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (9 files / 102 tests); `i18n:check` green (500 keys, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). The cancel-branch matrix was executed against a mirror of the shipped logic for all 5 sources × dev/prod. NOT device-verified — the floating pill wants an on-device look, and the promo-grant path wants one real dashboard grant end-to-end.
- Assumptions: (1) a complimentary/promotional grant is never user-cancellable — it is revoked from the dashboard, not the app. (2) Stripe/Amazon/Paddle/RC_BILLING/EXTERNAL/GALAXY are treated like promotional (no in-app cancel, entitlement preserved); none are configured today, so this is future-proofing rather than a live path. (3) Clearing a stale `localCancelled` in production is always the right repair, since the flag can no longer be legitimately set there.

### 2026-08-01 (c) - Redeem-code feature REMOVED; support emails now carry the RevenueCat App User ID
- What changed:
  - **Promo/offer-code redemption removed entirely** — product decision: there will be no redeem codes. Deleted the Settings → Support row, the Premium-screen "Redeem Code" button, `useSubscriptionStore.redeemCode()`, the transient `pendingRedeemRefresh` flag and its consumption in `refreshSubscriptionStatus`, and `RevenueCatSubscriptionService.presentCodeRedemption()` (which called Apple's offer-code sheet on iOS and opened `play.google.com/redeem` on Android). Also dropped the 3 now-dead i18n keys × 10 locales (`subscription.redeemCode`, `redeemFailedTitle`, `redeemFailedMessage`). Repo-wide grep for "redeem" in `src/`, `app/`, `scripts/` and `.maestro/` is now empty. **Complimentary Pro is now granted server-side only**, from the RevenueCat dashboard — which is exactly why the change below is needed.
  - **Both support emails now carry a diagnostics block** including the **Support ID = RevenueCat App User ID**. The app never signs users in, so RevenueCat identifies each install with an anonymous `$RCAnonymousID:…`; an incoming support email was therefore impossible to match to a RevenueCat customer, making a targeted promotional/complimentary Pro grant impossible. New `RevenueCatSubscriptionService.getAppUserId()` (returns `undefined` instead of throwing when billing is unconfigured — a support email must never be blocked by billing state) plus `buildSupportDiagnostics()` in `settings-screen.tsx`, appended to the body of BOTH "Leave feedback" and "Report a bug".
  - Block contents: `Support ID`, `App: SwipeClean <version>` (from `Constants.expoConfig.version`), `Platform: <os> <version>`, `Plan: <subscriptionStatus>`. No media, no filenames, no personal data — device/app metadata plus one opaque id. Preceded by "Please keep the lines below — they let us find your account." so users don't delete it.
- **Design decision — one block in both mails, NOT a third "Contact us" row.** The question was whether to add a separate section or fold the ID into the two existing entry points. Folding in wins: a third row is a third door to the same mailbox, and it would be the only one a user could reach WITHOUT the diagnostics attached — i.e. the exact case that can't be actioned. Feedback and bug report keep their distinct subject lines (`SwipeClean Feedback` / `SwipeClean Bug Report`) so they still sort in the inbox.
- Kept in English on purpose: the surrounding mail template was already English-only, and support is handled in English. Not an oversight — do not "fix" by localizing only the new block.
- To grant someone Pro: RevenueCat dashboard → Customers → paste the Support ID → grant the `CleanSwipe Pro` entitlement. The live `CustomerInfoUpdateListener` (`revenuecat.service.ts`) plus the `SubscriptionSync` foreground refresh (`app/_layout.tsx`) pick it up with no user action beyond reopening the app. NOTE the interaction with the known `localCancelled` issue in §10: a dashboard/PROMOTIONAL grant has `source` "none" and no `managementUrl`, so if that user had ever tapped Cancel Subscription, `reconcileEntitlement` will suppress the grant on every refresh. Worth fixing before relying on promo grants at volume.
- Files: `src/screens/settings-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/premium-screen.tsx`, `src/store/subscription-store.ts`, `src/features/subscription/revenuecat.service.ts`, `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (9 files / 102 tests); `i18n:check` green (500 keys, 9 locales in parity, 0 orphans — the 3 redeem keys were surfaced by the new orphan detector immediately after the UI was deleted); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). No Maestro flow referenced redeem, so no flow changes. NOT device-verified — the mailto body and `getAppUserID()` both want an on-device check.
- Assumptions: (1) no redeem codes are planned, so the code is deleted rather than feature-flagged — it is recoverable from git if that reverses. (2) Exposing the anonymous RevenueCat id to the user in their own outgoing email is acceptable; it is an opaque install identifier, not a credential, and it is already sent to RevenueCat on every request.

### 2026-08-01 (b) - Naming: the paid tier is "Pro" everywhere in the UI (was "Premium" in two places)
- What changed: the paid tier was presented inconsistently — the bottom tab read **"Premium"** while the screen it opened is titled **"SwipeClean Pro"**, and Settings offered "Upgrade to Premium". Every other user-facing string in the app already said Pro (`premium.title` = "SwipeClean Pro", `subscription.proTitle`/`proBadge`, `paywall.*`, `smartClean.unlock`, `advancedStats.unlock`, `reminders.pro*`). Exactly TWO keys were out of line, so this is a copy fix, not a rename:
  - `tabs.premium` → **"Pro"** in all 10 locales. Uses the Latin "Pro" everywhere, matching `subscription.proTitle`, which was already Latin "Pro" in hi/ar/ja. The previous values were localized/transliterated ("प्रीमियम", "بريميوم", "プレミアム") — the product name should not be transliterated when the rest of the paid-tier copy in those same locales is not.
  - `settings.upgradePremium` → the **existing, already-translated** value of `paywall.upgrade` in each locale ("Upgrade to Pro" / "Pasar a Pro" / "Auf Pro upgraden" / "Proにアップグレード" / …). Deliberately reused rather than writing 10 new translations, so the two upgrade CTAs can't drift apart again.
- **i18n KEY names were intentionally NOT renamed** (`tabs.premium`, `settings.upgradePremium`, the `premium.*` namespace) and neither was the **`/premium` route**. Key names and route ids are internal; the route id in particular is a registered deep link (`swipeclean://premium`) and `app/(tabs)/premium.tsx` is the same route for both tiers by design (§3). Renaming them would touch 10 locale files plus every call site for zero user-visible benefit and would break existing deep links.
- Maestro flows updated in the same pass — three flows asserted `text: "^Premium$"` on the tab and would have failed on the next run: `01_smoke.yaml`, `02_navigation.yaml`, `04_premium.yaml` now assert `^Pro$`. Verified `^Pro$` cannot collide with the plan badge: `premium-screen.tsx:176` renders `subscription.proTitle` through `.toUpperCase()`, i.e. "PRO", and Maestro text matching is case-sensitive. Flow `name:` "Premium upsell (Free user)" → "Pro upsell (Free user)", with `config.yaml`'s `executionOrder.flowsOrder` entry and the `.maestro/README.md` table updated to match (the flow name is the executionOrder join key — changing one without the other silently drops the flow from the ordering).
- Files: `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `.maestro/01_smoke.yaml`, `.maestro/02_navigation.yaml`, `.maestro/04_premium.yaml`, `.maestro/config.yaml`, `.maestro/README.md`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green; `npm test` green (9 files / 102 tests); `i18n:check` green (503 keys, 9 locales in parity, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings). Swept all 10 locales for the Latin string plus the three transliterations (प्रीमियम / بريميوم / プレミアム) — zero remaining user-facing "Premium". `store-listing-generated/LISTING.md` already said Pro throughout and needed no change. NOT device-verified.
- Assumptions: (1) "Pro" is the product name and should stay untranslated Latin in every locale, consistent with how `subscription.proTitle` already shipped. (2) The Free-user tab label should be "Pro" (what you get by upgrading) rather than "Upgrade" or "Go Pro"; the Pro-user label stays "Studio". (3) Remaining "Premium" occurrences in SOURCE are code comments referring to the `/premium` route and `PremiumScreen` component, which keep those names — left as-is on purpose.
- Follow-up for the store: the generated screenshot `store-listing-generated/08-pro.png` and the feature graphic were rendered from the old build, so any that show the bottom tab bar still read "Premium". Re-render before the next Play listing update.

### 2026-08-01 - Audit follow-ups: cancel-leak fixes, deletion-path unification, iCloud guard, Pro route guards, i18n orphan gate, README/AGENTS/CI
- What changed: acted on a full multi-agent re-audit of the repo (8 subsystem passes + adversarial verification of every doc claim against source). JS/config/docs only — no native-module or iOS-project changes in this pass.
  - **Cancelled jobs no longer write to the gallery.** Both job services take an `isCancelled()` callback and re-check it AFTER the encode and BEFORE any library write, then delete the temp artifact. Previously `saveToLibrary` ran before the store's cancelled-job guard, so cancelling a conversion still deposited the file in the user's gallery (and a cancelled compression could still orphan a copy). Closes the remaining half of SECURITY_SCAN M2. NOTE: the native encode still cannot be aborted mid-flight — this bounds the *side effects* of a cancel, it does not stop the work.
  - **All library deletions now route through `PhotoLibraryService`.** `deleteCompressedMediaCopy` called `MediaLibrary.deleteAssetsAsync` directly, bypassing the service that treats a `false` result (user denied the OS delete-consent dialog) as a FAILURE. The "single audited deletion path" claim in APP_ANALYSIS was true for the review queue and Smart Clean but not app-wide; it is now true for every library delete.
  - **iOS iCloud guard in Smart Clean.** `resolveReadableUri` (called for EVERY photo in the pre-pass) plus both `lacksCameraExif` helpers called `getAssetInfoAsync` without `{shouldDownloadFromNetwork:false}` — the exact opt-out `photo-library-service.ts` documents as necessary. A cold scan on an iCloud-optimized library would have downloaded the entire library over the network.
  - **Pro routes assert their own entitlement.** New `useRequireProFeature(featureKey)` hook; applied to `convert-run`, `convert-batch` and `smart-clean-review`. These are top-level expo-router routes and the app registers the `swipeclean://` scheme, so a Free user could previously deep-link straight past the Studio-screen gate. Waits for subscription-store hydration before redirecting so a cold deep-link launch never bounces a paying user to the paywall.
  - **Feature-cache persist is no longer O(n²).** `createJSONStorage(() => createDebouncedStorage(n))` only debounces the AsyncStorage WRITE — zustand-persist still ran `partialize` + `JSON.stringify` on every `set()`, i.e. once per asset over the whole cache during a scan. Added `createDebouncedObjectStorage<T>()` (a `PersistStorage`) that holds the raw object and stringifies once per trailing flush; the Smart Clean feature cache uses it. Its `migrate` now also normalizes a legacy blob missing `entries`/`order` instead of rehydrating them as `undefined`.
  - **Media index now participates in the background flush.** `createDebouncedIndexStorage` hand-rolled its own debounce and never registered a flusher, so up to 800 ms of index writes were dropped on suspend despite the comment in `app-store.ts` implying global coverage. Added `registerDebouncedFlusher()` and wired it.
  - **Deletion history is capped** at `HISTORY_CAP = 2000` (mirrors the cleanup-events ledger), applied on write and retro-trimmed on rehydrate. Closes SECURITY_SCAN L13.
  - **Ads release-safety.** `EXPO_PUBLIC_ADS_USE_TEST` is now documented in `.env.example` and `assertAdConfigIsProductionSafe()` logs a loud startup warning in any release build carrying it. Previously the only guard against shipping a $0 test-ads production build was a source comment.
  - **i18n orphan detection.** `scripts/check-i18n.mjs` now scans `src/`+`app/` for literal key references and reports keys in `en.json` that nothing uses, with a `DYNAMIC_KEY_PREFIXES` allow-list for runtime-composed keys and a `--strict-orphans` flag. Parity checking is structurally blind to dead strings, so they accumulated and were re-translated on every "one agent per locale" pass. Pruned **66 orphan keys × 10 locales = 660 strings** (569 → 503 keys), including the entire `policy.*` namespace for the never-built "after compression" screen and the `compression.banner*` namespace for a removed banner.
  - **Dead code removed:** `src/components/premium-card.tsx` (zero importers; its copy advertised a one-time upgrade that contradicts the shipped subscription model), the `afterCompressionOriginalPolicy` setting + its type (typed, defaulted and persisted with zero readers), and an unused `BarChart3` import in the Stats screen (the only non-advisory lint warning).
  - **Licensing correction:** the Open-Source Licenses screen claimed "GNU Lesser General Public License, version 2.1" for LAME, but the vendored `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/lame/COPYING` is the GNU **Library** General Public License, **version 2** (June 1991). Wrong license version on a legal-attribution screen; corrected in all 10 locales. (The license body is still English in every locale — legal text, deliberate.)
  - **Untranslated string:** `swipe-screen.tsx` rendered a hardcoded English "Loading your media library..." even though `common.loadingMediaLibrary` exists in all 10 locales and is used correctly 56 lines earlier.
  - **Repo hygiene:** `store-listing-generated/*.png` are now tracked (the blanket `*.png` ignore meant a fresh clone got `LISTING.md` and none of the 10 screenshots it describes); new root `AGENTS.md` pointing at §12 (which sits ~1,400 lines into a ~400 KB file with no root-level pointer); new `README.md` (the repo had none — every dev command lived only in §8, and neither git-ignored build secret was documented anywhere); new `.github/workflows/gates.yml` running all four gates (Node-only, no device or secrets needed — the repo had no CI at all).
  - **§10 rescoped to CURRENT-ONLY** with a Resolved footer. The "do not remove previous history" rule had been applied to §10 as well as §11, leaving four items warning about already-fixed problems (debug keystore, TEST rewarded unit, fail-open consent, R8 off). Stale §2/§9 wording describing consent as "fail-open" and ad-config as `__DEV__ ? TestIds : real` was corrected to match the code.
- Files: `src/features/convert/convert.service.ts`, `src/features/convert/convert.store.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression.service.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression.store.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression-deletion.service.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/detectors/shared.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/detectors/tier1-screenshots.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/detectors/tier3-memes.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/feature-cache-store.ts`, `src/features/subscription/use-require-pro-feature.ts` (new), `src/features/ads/ad-config.ts`, `app/_layout.tsx`, `src/screens/convert-run-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/convert-batch-run-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/smart-clean-review-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/swipe-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/stats-screen.tsx`, `src/store/app-store.ts`, `src/store/media-index-store.ts`, `src/utils/debounced-storage.ts`, `src/models/photo.ts`, `src/services/settings-service.ts`, `src/components/premium-card.tsx` (deleted), `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (10), `scripts/check-i18n.mjs`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, `AGENTS.md` (new), `README.md` (new), `.github/workflows/gates.yml` (new), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: typecheck green (`tsc --noEmit`, 0 diagnostics); `npm test` green (9 files / 102 tests); `i18n:check` green (503 keys, 9 locales in parity, 0 orphans); `npm run lint` green (0 errors, 92 warnings — was 93; the removed dead import was the only actionable one). NOT device-verified — per the standing division of labour the user runs on-device QA. Nothing in this pass touches native code, so no rebuild is required; Fast Refresh picks all of it up.
- Assumptions: (1) the 66 pruned i18n keys are dead — each was verified unreferenced in `src/`+`app/` under all three quote forms, and the 6-key second wave was surfaced by the new checker after `PremiumCard` was deleted, which is the cascade it exists to catch. (2) `afterCompressionOriginalPolicy` is not staged for an imminent screen; it had been typed/defaulted/persisted with zero readers since the 2026-06-15 foreground-compression redesign removed the batch flow. (3) Redirecting from a Pro route is preferable to rendering a paywall in place, matching what an in-app denial already does.
- Deliberately NOT done in this pass (needs a device, a Mac, or a product decision): SECURITY_SCAN **M6/M7/M8** (whole-track PCM buffered in RAM on Android; unbounded MediaCodec drain loops with no timeout or cancel; the non-contiguous `CMBlockBuffer` over-read on iOS) — all native, all needing real-device QA; **H1** iOS static-linked LAME (LGPL §6 relink problem — the attribution half is shipped, the linkage half is architectural); the iOS **privacy manifest** contradiction and missing `NSUserTrackingUsageDescription`; the missing `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`; the `supportsPictureInPicture` app.json↔AndroidManifest drift; and **offline entitlement expiry** (`expiresAt` is persisted but never compared — a naive fix revokes Pro from users in a billing grace period, so it needs a grace window and a product call, not a date compare).

### 2026-07-17 (b) - iOS AdMob unit IDs wired + meme-detector over-match fix (extracted to a pure, unit-tested classifier)
Follow-up to the same-day iOS pass, after the user supplied the iOS AdMob ad unit IDs.
- **iOS ad units wired** in `src/features/ads/ad-config.ts` (all `ca-app-pub-5256708773143000/…`): banner `3231384714`, interstitial `1238957839`, rewarded `7964836642`. `__DEV__` still forces Google `TestIds`; these serve only in release. The real iOS AdMob **App ID** `ca-app-pub-5256708773143000~9054968752` is now wired (`app.json iosAppId` + Info.plist `GADApplicationIdentifier`), so **iOS AdMob is fully configured** (App ID + 3 units + SKAdNetwork). Android already had its real App ID `~5412947073` (`app.json androidAppId` + injected into `AndroidManifest.xml` as `com.google.android.gms.ads.APPLICATION_ID`). AdMob is now complete on both platforms. **RevenueCat iOS `appl_` public SDK key wired (2026-07-18)** into gitignored `.env.local` (`EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`; value not stored in-repo) — so all iOS monetization CONFIG (AdMob + RevenueCat) is done for LOCAL builds. For **EAS cloud builds** the key must ALSO be set as an EAS env var (`EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_IOS_API_KEY`, plaintext, `production`) since `.env.local` isn't uploaded — EAS is not logged in on this machine, so the user must do it. Dev-sim still uses the Test Store (`USE_TEST_STORE=1`); the `appl_` key only applies in release/TestFlight (`__DEV__` false). iOS dashboard setup in progress by the user (ASC app + subscriptions created; RevenueCat App Store app + In-App Purchase `.p8` key configured; product/entitlement/offering mapping + "Ready to Submit" screenshot pending).
- **Meme detector over-match fixed** (the ~27/32-on-iOS finding). Root cause: the heuristic classified on TWO WEAK signals alone — "small image" + "no camera EXIF" — so any small, EXIF-less saved image/screenshot scored the 0.5 threshold; on iOS (IMG_#### names, no meme album) that flagged almost everything. Fix: a meme now REQUIRES a strong signal (meme-y filename or meme source album); the weak signals may only corroborate. Extracted the scoring into a NEW pure module `src/features/smart-clean/detectors/meme-classifier.ts` (`memeScore`/`isMeme`/`memeNeedsExifCheck`, native-free) with `meme-classifier.test.ts` (**+8 tests → 102 total**) that locks in "small + EXIF-less + no-name ⇒ not a meme" and preserves the lazy-EXIF perf gate. `tier3-memes.ts` now delegates to it. Gates: typecheck PASS, i18n parity, 0 lint errors (the advisory React-Compiler warning COUNT is nondeterministic run-to-run — 93↔186, all `warn`, unrelated to these files). NOTE: could not re-confirm the meme drop on the sim — the expo-dev-client kept serving a cached JS bundle (a fast-refresh/relaunch of a module-singleton detector doesn't reliably reload); the unit test is the authoritative verification. The compress result size-display finding is being handled in a separate spawned session (task_fb8e4aa8).

Applied the ID-independent iOS blockers from the 2026-07-17 parity audit (RevenueCat iOS key + real AdMob IDs still deferred to the user; LAME-off-iOS deferred to avoid a risky rebuild), then validated the three core flows on the iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 simulator via Maestro. All gates green after: `typecheck` PASS, `lint` 0 errors / 93 warnings, `i18n:check` **569 keys** (added `lock.useFaceUnlock` + `subscription.termsOfUse` across all 11 locales), `vitest` **94** pass.
- **Native config (rebuild-affecting).** `ios/SwipeClean/Info.plist`: `CFBundleShortVersionString` 0.1.0→**1.0.0**; removed the unused `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`; removed `UIBackgroundModes:audio`; added `SKAdNetworkItems` (38 Google/mediation IDs). `project.pbxproj` `MARKETING_VERSION` 1.0→1.0.0 (both configs). `package.json` 0.1.0→1.0.0. `SwipeClean.entitlements`: dropped `aps-environment:development` (no remote push). App icon **alpha flattened** (was RGBA → ITMS-90717 risk; now opaque `assets/icon.png` + the committed `App-Icon-1024x1024@1x.png`) and `app.json` gained an `icon` key + `expo-image-picker microphonePermission:false` + `expo-video supportsPictureInPicture:false` (so a future prebuild won't re-add the mic string / audio background mode). NOTE: the committed `ios/` is authoritative for EAS builds (EAS does not re-prebuild), so the direct plist/pbxproj/entitlements edits are what ship; the app.json flips are prebuild-parity only.

Both-platform bug fixes (help Android): `app-lock-gate.tsx` Android face-unlock label (was Apple "Use Face ID" unconditionally → `lock.useFaceUnlock`); `photo-preview-screen.tsx` Android share via `Sharing.shareAsync` (was `Share.share({message})` sharing a URI string). `convert.store.ts` error-classifier now catches `webm/gif/audio-*-unavailable`. ### 2026-07-16 - Smart Clean FGS notification: 10 s → 390 ms (RNBA patch) + 16 KB alignment VERIFIED + Play demo video recorded
- **ROOT CAUSE (measured on the S24, NOT what was first assumed): Android's 10-second foreground-service notification deferral.** Since Android 12 (API 31) the system DEFERS an FGS notification by exactly 10 s unless the notification opts out via `setForegroundServiceBehavior(FOREGROUND_SERVICE_IMMEDIATE)`. `react-native-background-actions` (RNBA) builds its notification with `NotificationCompat.Builder` and never sets it. Measured: **service up at 241 ms, notification only shown at 10,261 ms**. This ALSO explains "sometimes it never shows at all" — a scan that finishes inside the 10 s window tears the service down before the deferred notification is ever displayed.
  - **FIX: `patches/react-native-background-actions+4.1.0.patch`** (via `patch-package`, new devDependency + `"postinstall": "patch-package"`): adds `.setForegroundServiceBehavior(NotificationCompat.FOREGROUND_SERVICE_IMMEDIATE)` and raises `PRIORITY_MIN` → `PRIORITY_LOW` in `RNBackgroundActionsTask.java`. **VERIFIED: notification now visible at 390 ms (was 10,261 ms) — ~26× faster.** NOTE for patch regeneration: delete `node_modules/react-native-background-actions/android/{build,.gradle,.cxx}` first and pass `--exclude '(^|/)(build|\.gradle|\.cxx)(/|$)'`, or patch-package tries to diff gradle build artifacts and blows its buffer.
  - **Play FGS demo video recorded** (`swipeclean-foreground-service-datasync-demo.mp4`, 26 s, 1080×2340): Smart Clean → Scan now → notification appears immediately → app backgrounded → notification + progress persist (proves the dataSync use case). Unblocks the FOREGROUND_SERVICE_DATA_SYNC declaration.
- **The two fixes below were REAL gaps but were NOT the cause of the reported symptom** (the S24 already had `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` granted, and its index was warm). Kept because they harden fresh installs / cold-cache first runs: (1) the scan flow NEVER requested `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, so on Android 13+ the FGS notification would be silently SUPPRESSED on a fresh install (only the compression flow requested it); (2) on a cold cache the media-index `startFullScan()` ran in `smart-clean-screen.tsx handleScan` BEFORE `runScan()` posted anything — a silent gap that stacks on top of the 10 s deferral.
  - New `SmartCleanScanNotifications.ensurePermission()` requests notification permission on BOTH platforms; `handleScan` awaits it before starting. Auto-resume at launch does NOT prompt (only user-initiated scans do).
  - `runScan` refactored: the cold-cache `startFullScan()` now runs INSIDE the foreground-service-wrapped task (`scanBody` → `execute`), so the RNBA `dataSync` notification is up from the first moment and covers indexing; `handleScan` no longer builds the index itself. Token/abort/checkpoint discipline preserved (token re-checked after the index build); a synchronous `set({ phase: "scanning" })` keeps the UI flip instant.
  - Cross-platform: `SmartCleanScanNotifications.showProgress`/`dismiss` + `updateScanNotification` no longer early-return on iOS — iOS now gets the same ongoing scan notification (a plain local notification; iOS has no FGS). The scan notification is presented QUIETLY (`shouldShowBanner:false` for the scan type) so iOS doesn't re-pop a banner on every progress update. The RNBA `dataSync` foreground service itself stays Android-only (no iOS equivalent).
  - Files: `src/features/smart-clean/smart-clean-notifications.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/smart-clean-store.ts`, `src/screens/smart-clean-screen.tsx`. Green: typecheck / i18n:check / lint / vitest (94). NEEDS on-device verification (S24) that the FGS notification appears instantly once `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` is granted.
- **16 KB page-size alignment — DONE & VERIFIED.** Added `target_link_options(swipecleanlame PRIVATE "-Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384")` to `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/android/CMakeLists.txt`. NDK r27 (the RN 0.81 default, and what's installed here) does NOT 16 KB-align app-compiled libraries without it; r28+ makes it the default (flag then a no-op). `libswipecleanlame.so` is the ONLY custom-compiled Android `.so` (WebM/GIF/audio-extract are pure Kotlin/Swift). VERIFIED on a fresh signed release APK (versionCode 3): all **26** arm64-v8a `.so` report LOAD align `0x4000` — ours + RN core + every third-party prebuilt (react-native-compressor, google-mobile-ads, react-native-purchases, fresco) — and `zipalign -c -P 16 4` passes. **No dependency bumps required**; no longer an Android-15 blocker. See the Release Prep section.

NOTE the policy pass DROPPED "GIF" from the Android Convert copy (GIF is iOS-only in this build) and avoids superlatives / the word "Tinder". Category = **Tools**; suggested tags = cleaner / storage / file management / photo management / utilities. Screenshot prices ($2.99 / $14.99) are PLACEHOLDERS.

- **Google Play Console state:** app created (package `com.cognitix.swipeclean`), payments/merchant profile done. Subscription **`swipeclean_pro`** with base plans **`monthly`** + **`yearly`** (auto-renewing). Signed AAB uploaded to the **Internal testing** track; testers added; testers join via the internal-test opt-in link (a service account / person never gets an auto-email). Play App Signing is ON. Privacy-policy URL for App content = the netlify page.

- **RevenueCat state:** project + Google Play app (`com.cognitix.swipeclean`) configured; Android public key (`goog_…`) in `.env.local` with Test-Store OFF. Service account created in Google Cloud; **Google Play Android Developer API enabled**; Play permissions granted by inviting the service-account `client_email` under Play Console **Users and permissions** (KEY GOTCHA: Google Cloud IAM ≠ Play permissions; the 3 needed are View app info + View financial data/orders + Manage orders & subscriptions; a service account shows "invitation pending" forever, which is normal). Products **imported** (`swipeclean_pro:monthly` / `:yearly`), attached to entitlement **`CleanSwipe Pro`**, and mapped into the **`default`** offering as **Monthly (`$rc_monthly`)** + **Annual (`$rc_annual`)** packages.

- **CURRENT BLOCKER:** the app's Premium tab shows RevenueCat *"There is an issue with your configuration"*. Most likely causes, in order: (1) the on-device app is the **sideloaded release APK** (signed with the UPLOAD key) rather than the **Play-delivered** build (signed with Google's Play App Signing key) — Google Play Billing only fetches products for the Play-installed build; fix = uninstall sideloaded, install from the internal-testing link; (2) **product propagation delay** (newly created Play subs can take up to ~24h). Verify base plans are **Active** (not Draft).

- **Free access (reference):** for lifetime comps/testers use **RevenueCat → Customers → Grant promotional entitlement → `CleanSwipe Pro` → Lifetime** (no code, no quota). Google Play subscription **promo codes** only grant free-trial/discount periods — NOT lifetime; a shareable lifetime code would require a separate one-time in-app product (not built).

- **Permanent app identity changed** to `com.cognitix.swipeclean` (Cognitix = the publishing org) BEFORE the first Play upload, since Google locks the package on first upload. Applied across the whole repo: `app.json` (`android.package` + `ios.bundleIdentifier`), `android/app/build.gradle` (`namespace` + `applicationId`), the two Kotlin sources **moved** `…/java/com/swipeclean/free/` → `…/com/cognitix/swipeclean/` with updated `package` declarations (R/BuildConfig regenerate under the new namespace), the iOS project (`project.pbxproj` `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` ×2 + `Info.plist`), all 10 `.maestro` flows (`appId:`), and this doc. No hardcoded FileProvider authorities existed (all use `${applicationId}`), so nothing else needed touching.
- **Requires a native rebuild** to take effect (the running dev client keeps the old id until rebuilt); the renamed app installs as a SEPARATE app on-device from any old `com.swipeclean.free` install. RevenueCat's Android app + AdMob's Play link must use the new package. AdMob ad-unit/App IDs are NOT package-bound, so they stay valid.

### 2026-07-07 - Pre-production hardening: swipe-decision race, estimate-based deletion, banner consent
- **Swipe binds to the swiped card (fixes wrong-asset keep/delete).** `SwipePhotoCard` now passes its own `photo.id` to `onSwipe(action, photoId)` (the card is keyed by `photo.id`, so its closure is exactly what the user swiped), and `useAppStore.swipeCurrentPhoto(action, photoId?)` resolves that id from `mediaIndex.assetsById` instead of re-calling `currentPhoto()` after the 300ms fly-off. Previously a background full-scan page commit (re-sorts newest-first) or a newest-page refresh (inserts a fresh capture at index 0) during the animation could shift `visiblePhotos[currentIndex]` and mark/keep the WRONG asset. Back-compatible: no id ⇒ old `currentPhoto()` behavior. Files: `src/components/swipe-photo-card.tsx`, `src/store/app-store.ts`.
- **Compression compares against the REAL original size (fixes deleting the smaller original).** `verifyCompressedOutput` now reads the real original file size first and only falls back to the caller's `originalSizeBytes` when the file can't be read. That caller value comes from `getOriginalBytes()`, which returns a pixels-based ESTIMATE when the media index has no real size; an over-estimate let a compressed copy LARGER than the true original pass the "did it shrink?" gate, report bogus savings, and (since delete-original only needs `savedBytes>0`) allow deleting the smaller real original. File: `src/features/compression/compression.service.ts`.
- **Banner ads now honor UMP consent.** Added session store `src/features/ads/ads-consent-store.ts` (`canRequestAds`, default true/fail-open); `app/_layout.tsx` publishes the `AdsConsentService.gather()` result into it before SDK init, and `useAdsVisibility` ANDs it into `shouldShowAds`. The banner (`ad-banner.tsx`) reads that hook, so a user who denied consent in a regulated region now gets NO ads including the banner (previously only interstitial/rewarded preloads respected consent). Partly supersedes the §7 banner-consent risk. Typecheck + 94 vitest tests green.

### 2026-06-29 - Convert overhaul (cross-platform parity) + UI redesign — Phase 1 done
- **SCOPE CHANGE:** the app now targets **iOS + Android with feature parity** (the prior iOS-only rule is dropped). Native Swift + Kotlin are both in scope; build features as cross-platform Expo modules under `modules/` (capability-probed so a not-yet-built platform degrades). See `CONVERT_PLAN.md` for the full plan + native vendoring TODO (LAME for MP3, libvpx/libwebm for iOS WebM).
- **Source-format-aware targets** (`src/features/convert/convert-targets.ts`, fully rewritten + unit-tested): target lists now depend on the source's actual extension and **never offer the file's own format back** (no PNG→PNG, MP4→MP4). New `sourceFormat`/`sourceFormatLabel`. Targets expanded to image jpg/png/webp, video mp4/webm/gif, audio mp3/m4a/wav. `ConvertCapabilities` is now per-engine (`audioM4a/audioMp3/audioWav/webm/gif`); `getSelectableTargets` filters by what the build can actually run, so new formats appear automatically as each native module ships. A GIF source offers still-image targets + mp4 (mp4 gated on the gif engine); HEIC/HEIF and GIF→static-frame work NOW via the existing `expo-image-manipulator` engine (no native add).

Per-ITEM target state (not one shared target). Added Clear + kept Cancel (already on both run screens). Old `convert-format-picker.tsx` deleted.
- **Audio now actually plays:** `convert.service.ts` best-effort saves audio output to the device Music library (`MediaLibrary.createAssetAsync`, Android) so M4A/MP3/WAV are openable; iOS falls back to sandbox + Share. Run screen shows "Saved to your Music" when it lands.
- **Phase 3 native module written (needs device build):** `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode` (`SwipeCleanAudioEncode`, Swift + Kotlin). **WAV + M4A are pure platform APIs (ready);** MP3 path wired but `supportsMp3()` returns false until LAME is vendored, so the MP3 chip stays hidden. JS engines added: `engine/audio-engine.ts` (rewritten), `engine/webm-engine.ts` + `engine/gif-engine.ts` (probes + bridges for the not-yet-written `SwipeCleanWebm`/`SwipeCleanGif`).
- **Gates green:** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors), `npm test` (91 tests; convert-targets fully re-tested), `npm run i18n:check` (545 keys × 10 locales; new convert.* keys added). **NOTE: vitest 4 needs Node ≥20.19 — use `~/.nvm/versions/node/v22.15.0` (default Node 20.17 fails to load the vitest config).** On-device visual QA pending (user does device testing); native modules need a dev-client rebuild to activate.
- **PART 2 (same day) — native modules built + verified on both platforms:**
  - **MP3 via vendored LAME 3.100 (user-approved, LGPL):** official tarball vendored into `modules/swipe-clean-audio-encode/lame/` (libmp3lame + mpglib + hand-written `config.h`). Android: `CMakeLists.txt` + `lame_jni.c` → `libswipecleanlame.so` (built for all 4 ABIs); Kotlin `LameBridge` probes via `System.loadLibrary`. iOS: LAME C compiled directly into the pod; ObjC `SCLameEncoder` (AVAssetReader PCM → `lame_encode_buffer_interleaved`) called from Swift. **Both app builds verified**: Android emulator install + iPhone 17 Pro sim "Build Succeeded" (runtime encode QA pending). Three config traps burned into `config.h` comments + the `lame-mp3-vendoring` memory: missing `ieee754_float32_t` typedef; `#define HAVE_NASM 0` still links x86 asm (leave undefined); CocoaPods `../` paths silently break `public_header_files` (hence the duplicated self-contained `ios/lame/`).
  - **`SwipeCleanWebm` (Android)**: video→WebM/VP8 MediaCodec transcode over an EGL surface bridge + `MUXER_OUTPUT_WEBM`; compile-verified on device. v1 video-only (drops audio; Opus later). iOS WebM (libvpx) not approved/started.
  - **`SwipeCleanGif` (iOS)**: video→animated GIF via AVAssetImageGenerator + ImageIO (15 s / 10 fps / ≤150 frames / ≤480 px caps); code-complete + pod-installed, build unconfirmed. Android GIF blocked on vendoring an encoder. (gif→mp4 was dropped from scope; a GIF source converts to a still via the image engine.)

### 2026-06-27 - Gallery / "All Media" screen redesign (dense grid + multi-select)
- Rebuilt the selected-photos / "All Media" screen (reached via the gallery icon next to the month-filter pill on the Swipe screen) into a dense, edge-to-edge gallery grid: `GAP = 2`, responsive `numColumns = clamp(round(listW / TARGET_CELL=100), 3, 6)` (~4 on phones, more on tablets), square `cellDp`-sized thumbnails with no border/radius, rendered via `FlashList` (`contentContainerStyle` inset by `GAP/2`).
- Added long-press multi-select. Long-pressing a tile (`delayLongPress` 280, Medium impact haptic) enters select mode and seeds the selection; a full-screen `GestureDetector` overlay then owns touches (`scrollEnabled={!selectMode}` so the list never fights the pan). Selection works by TAP (`Gesture.Tap` hit-test → `toggleAt`) AND by DRAG-paint (`Gesture.Pan` paints the inclusive anchor→current range via `rangeIndices`, unioned onto the pre-drag snapshot), combined with `Gesture.Exclusive(pan, tap)`. A JS `setInterval` (`AUTO_MS` 30) edge auto-scroll steps `AUTO_STEP` 26 px while the finger is within `EDGE_ZONE` 76 of a list edge and re-hit-tests at the last finger position. Hardware Back exits select mode (consumes the event) instead of leaving the screen.
- `SelectionActionBar` replaces the header in select mode (X exit / live pluralized count / Trash). Delete collects the selected photos and calls the store's `markManyForDeletion` — items route into the existing deferred Marked-for-Deletion queue (NOT an immediate OS delete), with a success notification haptic, then exits. Added `StatsService.withSwipeBatch` to apply N swipes of one action in a single update.
- Right-edge month scrubber (`gallery-month-scrubber.tsx`), shown only when the scope spans >1 month (`spans.length > 1 && maxScroll > 1` and not in select mode): a `TRACK_WIDTH` 30 strip with an accent thumb that follows scroll via a shared value (no React re-render) and, while dragging, fast-scrolls the list and shows a floating month bubble (`labelForFraction` → `monthLabel`). `buildMonthSpans` collapses the filtered list into contiguous month spans.

Extracted native-free pure utils into `src/utils/gallery-grid.ts` (`buildMonthSpans`, `hitTestGridIndex`, `rangeIndices`) with 12 vitest cases. ### 2026-06-26 - Animated bottom tab bar + "pop" page transition
- Replaced the default tab bar with a custom `AnimatedTabBar` (`src/components/animated-tab-bar.tsx`): a single absolutely-positioned highlight pill (`PILL_WIDTH` 50 × `PILL_HEIGHT` 28, `borderRadius` 16) slides horizontally via a Reanimated `translateX` shared value to `state.index * tabWidth`, spring `{ damping: 22, stiffness: 240, mass: 0.7 }`. First layout jumps (a `didInit` ref) so the pill does not slide in from x=0; later index changes spring. Pill color is accent/dark-mode aware (`accent33`/`accent1F`); active icon/label use `theme.accent` (active icon `strokeWidth` 2.8 vs 2.2). Tab selection fires `Haptics.selectionAsync()` before navigating. `tabMeta()` keeps the existing routes/labels and swaps `premium` → `Wand2`/`tabs.studio` when `isPro`.
- Added a "pop" scene transition in `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` `screenOptions` (independent of the tab-bar pill): the incoming screen rises (`translateY` 16→0), zooms (`scale` 0.9→1) and fades (`opacity` 0→1) via `sceneStyleInterpolator`, `transitionSpec` `timing` duration 220 ms, `Easing.out(Easing.cubic)`. User-chosen feel: iOS, sliding highlight, subtle/quick; pop = rise+zoom+fade, moderate ~0.90→1, smooth/quick.
- Files: `src/components/animated-tab-bar.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.

### 2026-06-26 - Convert "Studio" media-format converter (Pro)
- Added a Pro media-format converter living inside a renamed tab-4 "Studio" screen (`src/screens/studio-screen.tsx`): a brand `AppHeader` over a segmented `[ Clean | Convert ]` control (Clean = the existing Smart Clean via `<SmartCleanScreen showHeader={false} />`; Convert = `<ConvertScreen />`). The Expo Router route name stayed `premium` (the `/premium` deep link is preserved); only the title/tab label/icon swap for Pro. `premium-screen.tsx` renders `<StudioScreen />` only when `isPro`, so the gate is structural (the Pro-only Studio tab) rather than a per-button `canUseFeature` check; the new `"mediaFormatConvert"` flag is the last entry in `PRO_FEATURES`. Paywall gains a "File conversion" bullet.
- Convert surface (`convert-screen.tsx`): browse/stage up to 5 files (`MAX_BATCH = 5`) with removable thumbnails, one target picker per media kind, a Convert CTA, and a "Recent converted" list (tap to view/share, trash via `resetCompletedJob`). Routing: exactly 1 convertible item → `/convert-run` (single foreground screen via `useCustomConvertStore`); 2–5 → `enqueueBatch` then `/convert-batch`. Both routes are registered `presentation: "card", gestureEnabled: false` (swipe-back disabled while converting). The serial FIFO runner persists to `swipeclean-convert-store` (debounced).
- Engine seam: callers only call `convertMedia(...)` / `getConvertCapabilities()`. Image → image (jpg/png/webp) via `expo-image-manipulator` (no rebuild); video → MP4 (H.264) via `react-native-compressor` `Video.compress` (already linked, no rebuild); video → M4A via the new local native module (gated, see below). Conversion is NON-destructive — no keep/delete-original decision and no App-Lock gate; image/video outputs are saved to the gallery (`MediaLibrary.createAssetAsync`), audio is written to the app docs dir and shared (`savedToFile`). There is no "did it shrink?" check (a format change may grow the file). New pure layers (targets/selectors) carry vitest cases.
- Files: `src/features/convert/**` (types, targets, store, service, selectors, utils, custom store, `engine/{conversion-engine,image-manipulator-engine,video-engine,audio-engine}.ts`, `components/{convert-format-picker,convert-progress}.tsx`, tests), `src/screens/{studio-screen,convert-screen,convert-run-screen,convert-batch-run-screen}.tsx`, `app/{convert-run,convert-batch}.tsx`, `app/_layout.tsx` (route registration), `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` + `app/(tabs)/premium.tsx`, `src/features/subscription/feature-flags.ts`, `src/screens/premium-screen.tsx`, `src/i18n/locales/*.json`.

### 2026-06-26 - FFmpeg + MP3 dropped from Convert
- Dropped FFmpeg and MP3 output entirely (user decision: "continue without MP3"). MP3 was the only target forcing FFmpeg — there is no clean MP3 encoder on iOS/Android without LAME/GPL — so removing MP3 let the whole FFmpeg path go. The audio target is `m4a` only (`ConvertAudioTarget = "m4a"`), and the converter ships with no GPL dependency. The final target set is `jpg`/`png`/`webp`/`mp4`/`m4a`.
- Remaining `mp3`/`ffmpeg` mentions in the tree are "No FFmpeg, no MP3" disclaimer comments (engine/service/type/screen + the native module sources) or doc text; the only `'mp3'` literal is in `bundle-warm.out`, an untracked build artifact, not active source.
- Files: `src/features/convert/**` (the dropped path; comments), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.

### 2026-06-26 - M4A audio-extract local Expo native module
- Added `modules/swipe-clean-audio-extract/**` (module name `SwipeCleanAudioExtract`, platforms apple + android) to extract a video's audio track into M4A. Android (Kotlin `AudioExtractModule.kt`) remuxes the existing AAC track losslessly with no re-encode (MediaExtractor → MediaMuxer; throws `no-audio-track` if none); iOS (Swift `AudioExtractModule.swift` + `SwipeCleanAudioExtract.podspec`, iOS 15.1) re-exports via AVAssetExportSession `AppleM4A`. OS frameworks only — no FFmpeg, no MP3. The async `extractAudio(inputUri, outputPath)` returns the output `file://` URL.
- `audio-engine.ts` probes the module via `requireOptionalNativeModule("SwipeCleanAudioExtract")` (returns null until compiled); `getConvertCapabilities()` surfaces this as `audioExtract`, and `getSelectableTargets`/`isTargetAvailable` HIDE the M4A chip until the module is compiled in. So the module requires a dev-client REBUILD to appear, and on any client built before it, video offers MP4 only.
- Status: Android compiles and a full APK built OK; iOS is UNVERIFIED at runtime (Windows dev machine — the Swift module/podspec are present and look correct but were never built/run). GOTCHA documented: a local Expo module's `android/build.gradle` MUST set `versionCode` + `versionName`, or it cascades into a misleading async-storage `compileSdk` error.
- Files: `modules/swipe-clean-audio-extract/**` (`expo-module.config.json`, Android Kotlin, iOS Swift + podspec), `src/features/convert/engine/audio-engine.ts`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.

### 2026-06-20 - Smart Clean permission-scope rescan reliability + cache reuse
- Fixed the full↔selected-media reconciliation race: permission/AppState/library events still coalesce, but a durable pending-rescan intent now survives across queued passes. The first pass immediately closes Smart Clean review/preview and clears every old detector result; whichever pass finishes a permission-matching media-index rebuild starts the replacement scan. An incomplete/error index leaves the intent pending for the next reconcile instead of silently losing the rescan.
- Selected-media replacements are detected by comparing the exact authorized ID sequence, including same-count swaps, rather than relying on Zustand array-reference identity. Smart Clean checkpoint signatures now also fingerprint the exact access level + ID scope.
- Permission-only hiding no longer prunes `swipeclean-smart-clean-feature-cache`. Returning to full access rebuilds category results over the full library but reuses MD5/dHash/blur/video features for unchanged assets via the existing per-asset `modKey`; only new/modified/evicted assets require expensive recomputation. Confirmed deletions still prune real removals and the cache remains FIFO-capped at 8,000 entries.
- Settings' AppState return now uses the shared queued media reconcile instead of only refreshing the permission label, so changes made in system Settings reliably rebuild the authorized scope.
- Android 14 defense in depth: the permission service now verifies the actual `READ_MEDIA_IMAGES`, `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO`, and `READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED` platform grants. This overrides a stale/missing Expo `accessPrivileges` report, which otherwise classified selected-media access as full and merged a few readable assets into the old full index without pruning it (full counts plus blank revoked thumbnails). A launch-time reconcile after store hydration also handles JS/Fast Refresh, where no AppState `active` transition fires.
- Smart Clean now matches Swipe/Compress when all media access is removed: detector cards/results are hidden behind the shared themed permission empty state, with Allow Access while the OS can prompt and Open Settings after permanent denial. Limited access remains a valid state and continues to show the selected-media notice plus scans only the authorized scope.
- Added `permission-reconcile.ts` + 4 Vitest cases covering queued intent retention/retry, same-count selected-media swaps, and full/limited exact-scope fingerprints; added `permission-utils.ts` + 4 cases for Android full/selected/single-type/denied grant resolution. Also supplied the React 19-required initial values for two existing Compress-screen refs so typecheck is clean after the pulled FlashList update.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` passed; `npm test` 36/36 passed; `npm run i18n:check` passed (459 keys × 10 locales); `npm run lint -- --quiet` passed with 0 errors. Native full→limited→full behavior remains for device verification through the live Metro tunnel.
- Files: `src/hooks/use-photo-library-sync.ts`, `src/features/smart-clean/{permission-reconcile.ts,permission-reconcile.test.ts,smart-clean-store.ts}`, `src/services/{permission-service.ts,permission-utils.ts,permission-utils.test.ts}`, `src/screens/{settings-screen.tsx,history-screen.tsx}`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`. No dependencies, native configuration, persisted schemas, routes, or translations changed.

Cleanup starts the scan after permission is available and displays an updating estimate pill from persisted scanner totals instead of calculating total savings from the rendered list.

- New commands/configuration: None. Verification command used: `npm.cmd run typecheck`.
- Assumptions made: Scanner pages of 60 assets plus a short yield between pages should reduce visible UI stalls while still completing full-library estimates in a reasonable time. Estimates should ignore media already known as compressed at scan start.
- Remaining TODOs or limitations: This is cooperative background JS/native async work, not a guaranteed Android OS background job. Real-device testing is still needed for very large libraries, video-heavy galleries, app backgrounding during a scan, and interactions with compression jobs.

Verification: `npm.cmd run typecheck` (exit 0), `node scripts/check-i18n.mjs` (all 9 locales in parity, 291 keys), and on-device check — switching Settings → Language to Spanish instantly localized the whole UI (tabs, headers, month selector, and date strings e.g. "07 jun 2026"), then reverted to System default.

Interpolation uses `{{var}}` placeholders, including an English-style `{{plural}}` helper (resolves to "" or "s"); some locales legitimately repeat `{{plural}}` for grammatical agreement. RTL (Arabic) text translates immediately, but full layout mirroring via `I18nManager.forceRTL` only applies after an app reload. The translation and screen-wiring work was fanned out across multi-agent workflows (one agent per language; one per file) and then parity/type-checked centrally.

RTL layout mirroring needs hands-on QA after reload. Date/number formatting relies on the device's Hermes Intl support. Other still-placeholder Settings actions (biometric auth, app lock, feedback, bug report, privacy policy, analytics) are unrelated and remain unimplemented.

### 2026-06-08 - Compression Reliability & Completion-Sheet Fixes
- What was added: Fixed a set of compression bugs. (1) Data-loss guard: `CompressionService.compress` no longer saves to the library itself; `compressMediaJob` now verifies the temp output, THEN saves via `CompressionService.saveToLibrary`, and FAILS the job if no `libraryAssetId` comes back — so an original can never be deleted without a durable compressed copy. (2) `deleteOriginal` in the store now refuses unless a verified, saved copy exists (`libraryAssetId`) and `savedBytes > 0`. (3) `markCompleted`/`markFailed` ignore already-cancelled jobs. (4) New `deferOriginalDecision` store action lets the completion sheet be dismissed without forcing a keep/delete (the decision stays available on the compression detail screen). (5) The completion bottom sheet is now dismissible (swipe-down gesture, backdrop tap, Android back) and wraps its content in its own `GestureHandlerRootView` (required for gestures inside an RN `<Modal>`); "View Compressed File" now hides the sheet before navigating so the viewer is actually visible. (6) The compression media viewer now shows the COMPRESSED output (`compressedMedia.outputUri` by `sourceId`) instead of the original. (7) Completion/failure notifications update in place (removed the dismiss→reschedule flicker). (8) Added a Cancel button to the compression detail status panel (wires the previously-unused `cancelJob`).
- Files changed: `src/services/compression-service.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression.service.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression.store.ts`, `src/features/compression/components/compression-complete-sheet.tsx`, `src/screens/compression-media-viewer-screen.tsx`, `src/screens/compression-detail-screen.tsx`, `src/services/notification-service.ts`, and `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- New dependencies: None. Verification: `npm.cmd run typecheck` (exit 0); app reloads cleanly on the connected device; Cleanup screen and estimates render.
- IMPORTANT pre-existing bug discovered (NOT yet fixed): On Android 13+ with "selected photos only" media access (`READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED` granted, `READ_MEDIA_IMAGES`/`READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` denied), compressing camera photos fails with `EACCES (Permission denied)` because `react-native-compressor` reads the raw file path (`/storage/emulated/0/DCIM/Camera/...`) which is not accessible under scoped storage. This surfaces as the generic "We could not compress this file" failure and currently blocks compression for non-selected media. Fix options: request full media access (guide user to system settings), and/or resolve a readable `content://` URI (copy to app cache via `expo-file-system` / `MediaLibrary.getAssetInfoAsync`) before handing the path to the compressor. Because of this, the completion-sheet fixes (5/6 above) were verified by typecheck/code review but not yet exercised end-to-end on-device (a successful compression is required to reach the sheet).
- Remaining TODOs / recommended (not done): localize service-layer error strings (the store/service still emit English); format-aware image output (currently always JPEG, dropping PNG/HEIC efficiency); limited image-compression parallelism; smarter/real size estimates; true native cancellation (`Video.cancelCompression`) rather than soft-cancel; and the scoped-storage read fix above.

### 2026-06-09 - Limited Media Access Reconciliation (Swipe/Cleanup/Stats) + Compression EACCES Handling
- Problem: On Android "selected photos only" (limited) access, `MediaLibrary.getAssetsAsync` returns ONLY the accessible/selected set (verified on-device: `accessPrivileges=limited`, `totalCount=45`, `hasNextPage=false`), but the persisted media index kept ~3159 STALE entries from a prior full-access grant and was never reconciled — so Swipe, Cleanup, and Stats all showed/operated on photos the app can no longer read (blank thumbnails, failed compression). Root cause: `startInitialMediaIndexScan` early-returned once `lastFullScanCompletedAt` was set, and `refreshNewestPage` only ever ADDS (additive merge), so the index was never pruned after an access downgrade.
- Fix (single source of truth — all screens derive from the media index): made the index access-aware. `media-index-store` gained a persisted `accessLevel` ("full"|"limited"); `startFullScan` now makes its skip/no-op decision AFTER fetching live permission and NEVER skips under limited access or across an access-level change (but still caches a redundant full-access rescan); on completion it prunes everything not seen and records `accessLevel`. `app-store` gained `reconcileMediaIndex(permission)`: under limited it raises the loader (if the index isn't yet limited-reconciled), runs a pruning full scan, awaits it, then prunes `markedForDeletion`/`reviewedPhotoIds` ghost entries — but only when an authoritative limited scan actually completed since it began (guards against reset/early-resolve). Under full it does a fast newest-page refresh + cached background scan. `loadInitialData` only trusts the cache for instant paint when it was built under full access AND access is still full; the persist `merge` shows the loader on the first frame when the last known permission was limited; `refreshPhotos` now clears the loader on all paths; `stats-screen` derives from an empty set while `loadingPhotos`. `refreshNewestPage` now recomputes `summary` so the Cleanup savings estimate stays consistent.
- Concurrency: `startFullScan` PURE-JOINS an in-flight scan (never preempts — preemption was found to make callers' awaits resolve early, corrupting the prune/loader lifecycle and risking a marked/reviewed-queue wipe). A module-level `forceRescanRequested` flag makes a forced caller that JOINS an in-flight scan still prevent that scan from skipping (honours "Estimate again" / forced reconciles without preemption); it is reset in the scan's `.finally` and in `resetIndex` so it can't leak a redundant rescan. The scan IIFE has a `.catch` that resolves as an error status (never rejects) so awaiting callers always clear their loaders.
- Also (compression EACCES, partial): `compression-service.compress` now re-resolves a fresh readable URI via `getAssetInfoAsync` before compressing, and `getFriendlyCompressionError` maps `EACCES`/permission-denied to an actionable "allow access to all photos in Settings" message instead of the generic failure.
- Files changed: `src/store/media-index-store.ts`, `src/store/app-store.ts`, `src/screens/stats-screen.tsx`, `src/services/compression-service.ts`, `src/features/compression/compression.store.ts`, `src/services/photo-library-service.ts` (temporary diagnostic added then removed), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` (exit 0). Reviewed in depth by THREE adversarial multi-agent review passes (round 1 found 9 real defects → fixed; round 2 found 3 regressions introduced by an initial force-PREEMPT approach → fixed by switching to pure-join; round 3 confirmed only a self-limiting performance leak → fixed). NOT yet verified end-to-end on a physical device this session: the Galaxy S24 kept dropping off USB and the only available emulator (an experimental Android 16 "16KB page" image, `sdk_gphone16k_x86_64`) hangs in `expo-media-library` `getAssetsAsync`, so media never loads there. Next session should reload on the S24 and confirm the index reconciles to the accessible set across Swipe/Cleanup/Stats.

### 2026-06-09 - First-Launch Permission Flow Fix (hydration crash + auto-prompt + Settings control)
- Problem: On a fresh install/download the app got stuck forever on "Loading your media library…" and NEVER asked for Photos/Videos permission. Root cause (confirmed on-device via temporary diagnostics): the `useAppStore` persist `merge(persistedState, currentState)` ran with `persistedState === undefined` on a clean install and did `persisted.settings` / `...currentState.settings` without a null guard, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read property 'settings' of undefined`. A throw inside `merge` makes zustand call `onRehydrateStorage`'s finishing callback with `state === undefined`, so `state?.setHasHydrated(true)` silently no-opped → `hasHydrated` stayed `false` forever → the Swipe screen's `!hasHydrated` loader never cleared and `loadInitialData` (gated on `hasHydrated`) never ran, so the permission check/request never happened.
- Fix (all in `src/store/app-store.ts` unless noted):
  1. Null-safe hydration: `merge` now defaults `const persisted = (persistedState ?? {}) as Partial<AppStore>` so fresh/malformed persisted state can't throw.
  2. Bulletproof gate: `onRehydrateStorage` now always clears the gate — `(state ?? useAppStore.getState()).setHasHydrated(true)` — and warns on error, so even a future hydration failure can't strand the UI on the loader.
  3. Auto-prompt on first launch: `loadInitialData` now, when `getMediaPermission()` returns `not-requested`, calls `requestMediaPermission()` (toggling `requestingPermission`) so the native Android Photos/Videos dialog appears automatically instead of dropping silently to the manual screen. It only auto-requests on `not-requested`, so it never re-pops after the user has chosen. `refreshPhotos`/background sync deliberately do NOT auto-request (no surprise dialogs while polling).
  4. Declined fallback UI already existed and now actually renders (the Swipe-screen `EmptyState` with an Allow Access button + Open Settings link) because hydration no longer hangs before it.
  5. Settings control: added a new "Permissions" section to `settings-screen.tsx` with a "Photos & Videos" row whose subtitle shows the live access level (Full / Limited / Not granted). Tapping it opens system settings when access is already granted (the only way to change/revoke), otherwise calls `requestPhotoPermission`. Added a lightweight read-only `refreshPermissionStatus()` store action (re-checks `getMediaPermission` and updates `permission`) that Settings calls on mount/focus so the displayed status stays fresh after returning from system settings.
- Files changed: `src/store/app-store.ts` (merge guard, robust onRehydrate, auto-request in loadInitialData, new `refreshPermissionStatus` action + type), `src/screens/settings-screen.tsx` (Permissions section + status logic), `src/i18n/locales/*.json` (5 new keys × 10 locales: `permissions.statusFull|statusLimited|statusNone`, `settings.permissions|mediaAccess`), `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`. Temporary diagnostics were added to `swipe-screen.tsx`/`app-store.ts` during investigation and removed.
- New dependencies: None. New commands/config: None.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck` (exit 0); `node scripts/check-i18n.mjs` (all 9 locales in parity, 296 keys). Verified END-TO-END on a physical device this session (Samsung Galaxy Tab A9+, SM-X400, serial `R52Y90CXRJW` — NOT the S24; the S24 was not connected): `pm clear` → relaunch reproduced the fresh-install path; the app now hydrates, the native "Allow SwipeClean Free to access photos and videos" dialog auto-appears, "Allow all" loads the swipe grid, and Settings → Permissions shows "Photos & Videos — Full access".
- Assumptions: Auto-requesting only on `not-requested` is the right trigger (respects a prior denial); changing an already-granted level must go through system settings (Android gives no in-app re-prompt for that). The new locale strings use an em-dash status format matching `en.json`.
- Remaining TODOs: The Settings "Photos & Videos" row, the limited-access selection, and the declined→re-grant path still want broader device QA (incl. permanently-denied `canAskAgain=false`, where Allow Access routes to system settings). The 2026-06-09 Limited Media Access Reconciliation work is now indirectly exercisable on this tablet (it was previously blocked by the stuck loader) but still merits the focused full→limited downgrade QA noted in that entry.
